# 2014 Gaza War

For other conflicts called the "Gaza War", see [Gaza War (2008–2009)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaza_War_\(2008%E2%80%932009\)), [2012 Gaza War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2012_Gaza_War), and [Gaza war](74998519.md).

The **2014 Gaza War**, also known as **Operation Protective Edge** ([Hebrew](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hebrew_language): מִבְצָע צוּק אֵיתָן, [romanized](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romanization_of_Hebrew): *Miv'tza Tzuk Eitan*, lit. 'Operation Strong Cliff'[^cite_note-29][^cite_note-30][^cite_note-31][^cite_note-32][^cite_note-33]), and **Battle of the Withered Grain** ([Arabic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arabic_language): معركة العصف المأكول, [romanized](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romanization_of_Arabic): *Maʿrakat al-ʿAṣf al-Maʾkūl*[^cite_note-34][^cite_note-35]), was a military operation launched by [Israel](9282173.md) on 8 July 2014 in the [Gaza Strip](12047.md), a [Palestinian](241405.md) territory [governed](38626632.md) by [Hamas](13913.md) since 2007 and which was [occupied by Israel since 1967](76465951.md) until its [disengagement in 2005](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_disengagement_from_the_Gaza_Strip).[^cite_note-36] Following the [kidnapping and murder of three Israeli teenagers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_Gush_Etzion_kidnapping_and_murder) in the [West Bank](33209.md) by [Palestinian](23267.md) militants, the [Israel Defense Forces](39237.md) (IDF) initiated *Operation Brother's Keeper*, in which it killed 10 Palestinians, injured 130 and imprisoned more than 600.[^cite_note-Maannews1-37][^cite_note-nathanthrall-38][^cite_note-39][^cite_note-40] Hamas reportedly did not retaliate but [resumed rocket attacks on Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Palestinian_rocket_attacks_on_Israel_in_2014) more than two weeks later, following the killing of one of its militants by an Israeli airstrike on 29 June.[^cite_note-Sharon-41] This escalation triggered a seven-week-long conflict between the two sides, one of the deadliest outbreaks of [open conflict between Israel and the Palestinians](46216.md) in decades. The war resulted in over two thousand deaths, the vast majority of which were Gazan Palestinian civilians.[^cite_note-42] This includes a total of six Israeli civilians who were killed as a result of the conflict.[^cite_note-43]

The Israeli military operation aimed to stop rocket fire into Israel from the Gaza Strip. Conversely, Hamas' attacks aimed to bring international pressure onto Israel with the strategic goal of forcing the latter to lift the [naval and air blockade of the Gaza Strip](19224310.md); among its other goals were to end Israel's attacks on Palestinians, obtain a third party to monitor and guarantee compliance with a ceasefire,[^cite_note-gaza-ceasefire-44] release [Palestinian political prisoners](18158609.md) and overcome its isolation.[^cite_note-45] According to the *[BBC](19344654.md)*, Israel launched airstrikes on the Gaza Strip in retaliation to the rocket attacks by Hamas, [Palestinian Islamic Jihad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic_Jihad_Movement_in_Palestine) (PIJ), and other [Palestinian militant groups](2646043.md).[^cite_note-46]

On 7 July, after seven Hamas militants died in a tunnel explosion in [Khan Yunis](101330.md) that was caused either by an Israeli airstrike (per Hamas, [Nathan Thrall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nathan_Thrall), *BBC*, and a senior IDF official)[^cite_note-47] or an accidental explosion of their own munitions (per the IDF), Hamas assumed responsibility for rockets fired into Israel, and subsequently launched 40 more rockets towards Israel.[^cite_note-48][^cite_note-49] The Israeli aerial operation officially began the following day, and on 17 July, it was expanded to include a full-scale ground invasion of the Gaza Strip with the stated aim of destroying [Gaza's tunnel system](43413500.md);[^cite_note-50] the Israeli ground invasion ended on 5 August.[^cite_note-withdrawal2-51] On 26 August, an open-ended ceasefire was announced.[^cite_note-52] By this time, the IDF reported that Hamas, PIJ, and other Palestinian militant groups had fired 4,564 rockets and [mortars](324499.md) into Israel, with over 735 projectiles having been intercepted mid-flight and shot down by Israel's [Iron Dome](18342427.md). Most Gazan mortar and rocket fire was inaccurate, and consequently hit open land; more than 280 projectiles had landed within the Gaza Strip,[^cite_note-53][^cite_note-54][^cite_note-failing_rockets-55] and 224 had struck residential areas.[^cite_note-ynetnumbers-56][^cite_note-57] Palestinian rocketry also killed 13 Palestinian civilians in Gaza, 11 of them children.[^cite_note-58][^cite_note-AMINT-59] The IDF attacked 5,263 targets in the Gaza Strip; at least 34 known tunnels were destroyed[^cite_note-ynetnumbers-56] and two-thirds of Hamas's 10,000-rocket arsenal was either used up or destroyed.[^cite_note-60][^cite_note-61]

Between 2,125[^cite_note-MFA-report-fatalities-23] and 2,310[^cite_note-Ma'an2015Jan-19] Gazans were killed during the conflict while between 10,626[^cite_note-Ma'an2015Jan-19] and 10,895[^cite_note-PCHR-62] were wounded (including 3,374 children, of whom over 1,000 were left permanently disabled).[^cite_note-63] Gazan civilian casualty estimates range between 70 percent by the [Gaza Health Ministry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Health,_Palestine),[^cite_note-OCHA-13][^cite_note-jihad121-20][^cite_note-PCHR-62] 65 percent by the [United Nations](31769.md)' (UN) Protection Cluster by [OCHA](1602284.md) (based in part on Gaza Health Ministry reports),[^cite_note-HRC_REPORT-21] and 36 percent by Israeli officials.[^cite_note-partten-64][^cite_note-MFA-report-fatalities-23] The UN estimated that more than 7,000 homes for 10,000 families were razed, together with an additional 89,000 homes damaged, of which roughly 10,000 were severely affected by the bombing.[^cite_note-ynetnews.com-65] Rebuilding costs were calculated to run from [US\$](18717338.md)4–6 billion over the course of 20 years.[^cite_note-haaretz.com-66] 67 Israeli soldiers, 5 Israeli civilians (including one child)[^cite_note-67] and one [Thai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel%E2%80%93Thailand_relations) civilian were killed[^cite_note-OCHA-13] while 469 Israeli soldiers and 261 Israeli civilians were injured.[^cite_note-OPE-Israeli-wounded-17] On the Israeli side, the economic impact of the operation is estimated to have had an impact of [₪](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_new_shekel)8.5 billion (approximately US\$2.5 billion) and [a GDP loss of 0.4 percent](14690.md).[^cite_note-IsraelLoss-68]

## Background

Main article: [Gaza–Israel conflict](16346266.md)



Histogram of Palestinian rocket attacks on Israel per day and start of the military operation (in red), 2014

&#x20;In 2005, following the conclusion of the [Second Intifada](71717.md), Israel [disengaged from](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_disengagement_from_Gaza) the Gaza Strip, which it had previously occupied since 1967. The unilateral withdrawal plan began in August 2005 and was completed in September 2005.[^cite_note-69] Nonetheless, the [ICRC](5023832.md),[^cite_note-70] the [UN](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UN)[^cite_note-71] and various human rights organizations[^cite_note-72][^cite_note-73][^cite_note-74] consider Israel still to be the *de facto* occupying power due to its control of Gaza's borders, air space and territorial waters.[^cite_note-75][^cite_note-76]

In 2006, Hamas won [a majority of seats in the Palestinian legislative elections](38945340.md). This outcome surprised Israel and the United States who had anticipated the return of the Fatah opposition to power and, together with the [Quartet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quartet_on_the_Middle_East), they demanded Hamas accept all previous agreements, recognize Israel's right to exist, and renounce violence.[^cite_note-RoseVF-77] When Hamas refused, stating that the demand left it "handcuffed",[^cite_note-78] Israel [cut off aid to the Palestinian Authority](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2006%E2%80%9307_economic_sanctions_against_the_Palestinian_National_Authority). In mid-2006, [an Israeli soldier was captured by Hamas in a cross-border raid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2006_Gaza_cross-border_raid). The United States and Israel, in response to [Fatah](11104.md) moves in October 2006 to form a unity government with Hamas, tried to undo the elections by arming and training Fatah to overthrow Hamas in Gaza.[^cite_note-79] In June 2007, Hamas [took complete power of Gaza by force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Gaza_\(2007\)).[^cite_note-80][^cite_note-81][^cite_note-82][^cite_note-83][^cite_note-RoseVF-77]

Israel then defined Gaza as a "hostile territory" forming no part of a sovereign state and [put Gaza under a comprehensive economic and political blockade](19224310.md),[^cite_note-84] which also denied access to a third of its arable land and 85% of its fishing areas. It has led to considerable economic damage and humanitarian problems in Gaza.[^cite_note-85][^cite_note-86][^cite_note-87][^cite_note-88] The overwhelming consensus of international institutions is that the blockade is a form of collective punishment and illegal.[^cite_note-89][^cite_note-90][^cite_note-91][^cite_note-92][^cite_note-93] Israel maintains that the blockade is legal and necessary to limit [Palestinian rocket attacks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian_rocket_attacks_on_Israel) from the Gaza Strip on its cities and to prevent Hamas from obtaining other weapons.[^cite_note-94][^cite_note-95][^cite_note-96][^cite_note-97][^cite_note-98] Israel carried out [Operation Cast Lead](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Cast_Lead) in December 2008 with the stated aim of stopping rocket attacks from Hamas militants.[^cite_note-99] The [UN Fact Finding Mission on the Gaza Conflict](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Fact_Finding_Mission_on_the_Gaza_Conflict) concluded that the operation was "a deliberately disproportionate attack designed to punish, humiliate and terrorize a civilian population, radically diminish its local economic capacity both to work and to provide for itself, and to force upon it an ever increasing sense of dependency and vulnerability".[^cite_note-100] The Israeli government's analysis concludes that the report perverts international law to serve a political agenda and sends a "legally unfounded message to states everywhere confronting terrorism that international law has no effective response to offer them".[^cite_note-101]

### First Hamas–Fatah reconciliation (2011)

Influenced in the [Arab Spring](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arab_Spring) and by demonstrations in [Ramallah](44132.md) and Gaza, the gap between Hamas and Fatah was [bridged](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fatah-Hamas_conflict#April_2011_Cairo_agreement) in 2011. After the Palestinian president [Mahmoud Abbas](232595.md) declared his willingness to travel to Gaza and sign an agreement, the IDF killed two Hamas activists in Gaza; the IDF stated the killings were in response to the launching of a single Qassam rocket, which hit no one, but [Yedioth Ahronoth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yedioth_Ahronoth)'s Alex Fishman argued they were a "premeditated escalation" by Israel.[^cite_note-102] In an interview with [CNN](62028.md), Israeli prime minister [Benjamin Netanyahu](56472.md) declared that the reconciliation talks were calls for Israel's destruction, and strongly opposed the idea of a unity government.[^cite_note-103]

### November 2012 ceasefire and its violations

On 14 November 2012, Israel launched [Operation Pillar of Defence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Pillar_of_Defence) in the Gaza Strip. The operation was preceded by a period with a number of mutual Israeli–Palestinian responsive attacks.[^cite_note-104] According to the Israeli government, the operation began in response to the launch of over 100 rockets at Israel during a 24-hour period[^cite_note-105] and an explosion caused by [IEDs](265112.md), which occurred near Israeli soldiers, on the Israeli side of a tunnel passing under the [Israeli West Bank barrier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_West_Bank_barrier).[^cite_note-106][^cite_note-107] The Israeli government stated that the aims of the military operation were to halt rocket attacks against civilian targets originating from the Gaza Strip[^cite_note-108] and to disrupt the capabilities of militant organizations.[^cite_note-109] The Palestinians blamed the Israeli government for the upsurge in violence, accusing the IDF of attacks on Gazan civilians in the days leading up to the operation.[^cite_note-110] They cited the [blockade of the Gaza Strip](19224310.md) and the occupation of [West Bank](33209.md), including [East Jerusalem](343508.md), as the reason for rocket attacks.[^cite_note-111] A week later, on 21 November, Egypt brokered a ceasefire to the conflict which contained the following agreements:[^cite_note-112][^cite_note-113]

1. Israel should stop all hostilities in the Gaza Strip land, sea and air, including incursions and targeting of individuals.

2. All Palestinian factions shall stop all hostilities from the Gaza Strip against Israel, including rocket attacks and all attacks along the border.

3. The crossings should be opened, facilitating the movement of people and goods; Israel should refrain from restricting residents' movements and from targeting residents in border areas; procedures of implementation should be dealt with 24 hours after the start of the ceasefire.



[Gaza Strip](12047.md): access and closure



A sculpture in [Sderot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Palestinian_rocket_attacks_on_Israel,_2013#March) made from rocket debris

#### Violations

Both Israel and Hamas argue that the other violated the [2012 ceasefire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2012_ceasefire) agreement, resulting in 1 Israeli and 8 Gazan deaths and 5 Israeli and 66 Gazan injuries. According to the Israeli Security Agency ([Shabak](311752.md)) there was a sharp decrease in attacks from Gaza in 2013.[^cite_note-Shabak2013-114] Nevertheless, 63 rockets (average 5 per month) were launched in 36 rocket attacks in addition to various mortar attacks, all prohibited by the November 2012 ceasefire. The Palestinian Centre for Human Rights (PCHR)[^cite_note-PCHR2013-115] reported monthly Israeli attacks involving drones, missiles, small arms fire and airstrikes. Six of the deaths in Gaza occurred in the border area's Access Restricted Areas (ARAs, non-demarcated zones within Gazan territory unilaterally defined by Israel as being of restricted access), despite the ceasefire's prohibition on Israeli attacks on these areas.[^cite_note-nathanthrall-38][^cite_note-PCHR2013-115] OCHAO, more broadly sourced data, reported 11 deaths in Gaza and 81 injuries for 2013.[^cite_note-116]

In the first three months after the IDF [Operation Pillar of Defense](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Pillar_of_Defense), according to Ben White, two mortar shells struck Israeli territory, while four Gazans were shot dead and 91 were wounded by Israeli forces who fired inside Gazan territory on 63 occasions, made 13 incursions into the Strip, and attacked the Gazan fishing fleet 30 times.[^cite_note-117] Israeli attacks on Gaza steadily increased during the second half of 2013, notwithstanding the decrease in attacks from Gaza.[^cite_note-gaza-ceasefire-44]

From December 2012 to late June/early July 2014, Hamas did not fire rockets into Israel, and tried to police other groups doing so.[^cite_note-Goldberg1-118] These efforts were largely successful; Netanyahu stated in March 2014 that the rocket fire in the past year was the "lowest in a decade."[^cite_note-nathanthrall-38][^cite_note-Goldberg1-118][^cite_note-119] According to [Shabak](311752.md), in the first half of 2014 there were 181 rocket attacks[^cite_note-120] compared to 55 rocket attacks in whole 2013.[^cite_note-Shabak2013-114]

As occasional rocket fire continued, the blockade of Gaza continued in direct violation of the ceasefire agreement.[^cite_note-economist-two-big-121] "Crossings were repeatedly shut and buffer zones were reinstated. Imports declined, exports were blocked, and fewer Gazans were given exit permits to Israel and the West Bank."[^cite_note-nathanthrall-38]

Israel halted construction material going to Gaza after it stated that it had discovered a tunnel leading into Israel, some 300 m from a [kibbutz](106200.md). The IDF said it was the third tunnel discovered that year and that the previous two were packed with explosives.[^cite_note-122]



Some of the weapons captured in Khan Yunis

According to the Israeli Ministry of Foreign Affairs, there were 85 rocket attacks in the first five months of 2014.[^cite_note-123] Most of the 85 rockets were fired in March, after the IDF killed 3 members of Islamic Jihad. The members of the PIJ say they were firing rockets in response to an incursion by Israeli tanks and bulldozers into Gazan territory east of the Khan Yunis area.[^cite_note-124][^cite_note-125][^cite_note-126] The IDF said they were conducting routine military patrols near the Gaza border when they came under fire, and thus responded with airstrikes.[^cite_note-127][^cite_note-128]

### Second Hamas–Fatah reconciliation

See also: [Palestinian Government of 2014](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian_Government_of_2014)

Leading up to the collapse of the [2013–14 Israeli–Palestinian peace talks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2013%E2%80%9314_Israeli%E2%80%93Palestinian_peace_talks), in the face of Netanyahu's perceived reluctance to make desired concessions, [Mahmoud Abbas](232595.md) decided to forge a deal with Hamas.[^cite_note-Sharon-41] With its alliance with Syria and Iran weakened, the [loss of power by the Muslim Brotherhood in Egypt after a coup d'ètat in Egypt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2013_Egyptian_coup_d'%C3%A9tat), and the economic impact of the closure of its Rafah tunnels by [Abdel Fattah el-Sisi](36703624.md),[^cite_note-Sharon-41] on 23 April 2014, ending seven divisive years, Hamas agreed to reconciliation under a unity government with the other main Palestinian faction, [Fatah](11104.md).[^cite_note-129][^cite_note-130] The government accepted by Hamas was to be run exclusively by PNA technocrats.[^cite_note-Sharon-41]

This [Palestinian unity government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian_government_of_2014) was sworn in by 2 June 2014[^cite_note-131][^cite_note-Deal-132] and Israel announced it would not negotiate any peace deal with the new government and would push punitive measures.[^cite_note-133] Netanyahu took Palestinian unity as a threat rather than an opportunity.[^cite_note-Sharon-41][^cite_note-Heyer-134] On the eve of the agreement he stated that the proposed reconciliation would "strengthen terrorism", and called on the international community to avoid embracing it.[^cite_note-135] Most of the outside world, including the European Union, Russia, China, India, Turkey, France and the United Kingdom, proved cautiously optimistic, and subsequently expressed their support for new arrangement. The United States, more skeptical, announced it would continue to work with the PNA-directed unity government.[^cite_note-136] Israel itself suspended negotiations with the PNA[^cite_note-137] and, just after[^cite_note-138] the announcement, launched an airstrike, which missed its target and wounded a family of three bystanders.[^cite_note-Deal-132][^cite_note-139] Netanyahu had warned before the deal that it would be incompatible with Israeli–Palestinian peace and that Abbas had to choose between peace with Hamas and peace with Israel. When a reconciliation deal was signed, opening the way to the appointment of the new government, Netanyahu chaired a security cabinet which voted to authorise Netanyahu to impose unspecified sanctions against the Palestinian Authority.[^cite_note-Deal-132]

On 4 June, the day before [Naksa Day](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Naksa_Day), the Israeli Housing and Construction Ministry published tenders for 1,500 settlement units in the West Bank and East Jerusalem in a move Minister [Uri Ariel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Uri_Ariel) said was an "appropriate Zionist response to the Palestinian terror government."[^cite_note-140][^cite_note-141] [Marwan Bishara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marwan_Bishara), senior political analyst at *[Al Jazeera](30204052.md)*, alleged that Israel had hoped to disrupt the [Palestinian national unity government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian_government_of_2014) between Fatah and Hamas by its operation.[^cite_note-142]

### Immediate events



Factory bursts in flames after rocket attack in [Sderot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sderot), Israel, 28 June 2014.

[^cite_note-143]

On 12 June 2014, [three Israeli teenagers were abducted in the West Bank](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_kidnapping_and_murder_of_Israeli_teenagers): Naftali Fraenkel, Gilad Shaer, and Eyal Yifrah. Israel blamed [Hamas](13913.md), with Israeli prime minister Benjamin Netanyahu saying that he had "unequivocal proof" that Hamas was involved and that the abduction was linked to Palestinian reconciliation,[^cite_note-Sharon-41] and the IDF stated that the two men Israel suspected of having kidnapped the teenagers were known members of Hamas.[^cite_note-144][^cite_note-145] No evidence of Hamas involvement was offered by Israeli authorities at the time.[^cite_note-nathanthrall-38][^cite_note-Sharon-41][^cite_note-146] High-ranking members of Hamas denied the group had any involvement in the incident,[^cite_note-20140615TOI-147] and ex-[Shin Bet](311752.md) chief [Yuval Diskin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yuval_Diskin) doubted Hamas had any involvement.[^cite_note-Heyer-134] The Palestinian Authority in the West Bank attributed the abductions to the [Qawasameh clan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qawasameh_tribe), notorious for acting against Hamas's policies and any attempts to reach an [entente](https://en.wiktionary.org/wiki/entente) with Israel.[^cite_note-148] Hamas political chief Khaled Meshal said he could neither confirm nor deny the kidnapping of the three Israelis, but congratulated the abductors.[^cite_note-149] The kidnappings were condemned by human rights organizations.[^cite_note-150][^cite_note-151] Documents released by Israel suggest that Hamas member Hussam Qawasmeh organized the kidnappings with \$60,000 provided by his brother Mahmoud through a Hamas association in Gaza, after requesting support for a "military operation".[^cite_note-152] On 20 August, Saleh al-Arouri, an exiled Hamas leader based in Turkey, claimed responsibility for the kidnapping of the three Israeli teens: "Our goal was to ignite an [intifada](https://en.wiktionary.org/wiki/intifada) in the West Bank and Jerusalem, as well as within the 1948 borders... Your brothers in the [Al-Qassam Brigades](1703333.md) carried out this operation to support their imprisoned brothers, who were on a hunger strike... The [mujahideen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mujahideen) captured these settlers in order to have a swap deal."[^cite_note-153] Palestinian security forces said the kidnappings were organized by Saleh al-Arouri.[^cite_note-154] Khalid Meshaal, head in exile of Hamas's political wing since 2004, acknowledged that Hamas members were responsible, but stated that its political leaders had no prior knowledge of the abduction, were not involved in military details and learnt of it through the ensuing Israeli investigations. He also said that while Hamas was opposed to targeting civilians, he understood that Palestinians "frustrated with oppression" were exercising a "legitimate [right of resistance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Right_to_resist)" against the occupation by undertaking such operations.[^cite_note-155][^cite_note-156][^cite_note-157] Israel states that the IDF and the Shin Bet have foiled between 54[^cite_note-158] and 64 kidnapping plots since 2013. The PA said it had foiled 43 of them.[^cite_note-159]

Withholding evidence in its possession suggesting that the teens had been killed immediately until 1 July,[^cite_note-Sharon-41][^cite_note-Goldberg1-118][^cite_note-160] Israel launched [Operation Brother's Keeper](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Brother's_Keeper), a large-scale crackdown of what it called Hamas's terrorist infrastructure and personnel in the West Bank,[^cite_note-161] ostensibly aimed at securing the release of the kidnapped teenagers. During the operation, 11 Palestinians were killed and 51 wounded in 369 Israeli incursions into the West Bank through to 2 July,[^cite_note-162][^cite_note-Maannews1-37][^cite_note-163][^cite_note-164][^cite_note-165] and between 350 and 600 Palestinians,[^cite_note-20140615TOI-147][^cite_note-Maannews1-37][^cite_note-166][^cite_note-167] including nearly all of Hamas's West Bank leaders,[^cite_note-168][^cite_note-169][^cite_note-170] were arrested.[^cite_note-171][^cite_note-172][^cite_note-173] Among those arrested were many people who had only recently been freed under the terms of the [Gilad Shalit prisoner exchange](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gilad_Shalit_prisoner_exchange).[^cite_note-174] Israeli military spokesman Lt. Col. [Peter Lerner](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peter_Lerner) defended the arrests, stating that Hamas members had carried out 60 abduction attempts on Israelis in the West Bank "in the last year and a half", and that "Hamas does not need to give a direct order."[^cite_note-175] The arrests yielded no information about the abduction.[^cite_note-Sharon-41] [Amnesty International](18947898.md) and [Human Rights Watch](46530.md) stated that certain aspects of the operation amounted to collective punishment,[^cite_note-176][^cite_note-177] and B'tselem said in a press release that the actions have caused "disproportionate harm to the basic rights of Palestinians".[^cite_note-178] During the course of the operation, Israel said it had uncovered a Hamas plot to launch a massive wave of violence throughout the West Bank, with the goal of overthrowing the Palestinian Authority. The purported coup plotters were arrested and their weapons stockpiles were seized.[^cite_note-179][^cite_note-180]

On 30 June, search teams found the bodies of the three missing teenagers near Hebron.[^cite_note-181][^cite_note-182][^cite_note-183] After their burial, an anti-Arab riot broke out, and a [Palestinian teenager was murdered in revenge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kidnapping_and_murder_of_Mohammed_Abu_Khdeir). His killing sparked Arab rioting.[^cite_note-184][^cite_note-185] Israel police arrested six suspects belonging to the [Beitar Jerusalem F.C.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beitar_Jerusalem_F.C.) supporters' group [La Familia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/La_Familia_\(Beitar_supporters'_group\))[^cite_note-186][^cite_note-187] and charged three of them with murder.[^cite_note-188][^cite_note-189]

As part of its crackdown and concurrent to rocket fire from Gaza, Israel conducted air strikes against Hamas facilities in the Gaza Strip. Hamas apparently refrained from retaliating, though it did not impede other factions from firing rockets towards Israel.[^cite_note-Sharon-41] From 1 May to 11 June, six rockets and three mortar shells were launched from Gaza towards Israel. From 12 to 30 June 44 rockets and 3 mortar shells were launched from Gaza. On 29 June, an Israeli airstrike on a rocket crew killed a Hamas operative, while at least 18 rockets were launched from Gaza through the next day by Hamas according to both [J.J. Goldberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/J.J._Goldberg) and Assaf Sharon,[^cite_note-Sharon-41] with Goldberg stating that it was the first time Hamas directly launched rockets since the conflict in 2012.[^cite_note-Goldberg1-118] Overnight, on 30 June – 1 July, Israeli airstrikes struck 34 Gaza targets in what officials stated was a response to the Sunday rocketry,[^cite_note-190] while Stuart Greer reported the strikes were revenge for the deaths of the three youths.[^cite_note-191] From the day of the abductions on 12 June through 5 July 117 rockets were launched from Gaza and there were approximately 80 Israeli airstrikes on Gaza.[^cite_note-192][^cite_note-193]

Israel sought a ceasefire but refused to accept Hamas's condition that Palestinians arrested in the West Bank crackdown be released.[^cite_note-Sharon-41] In a meeting held on 2 July to discuss the crisis, Hamas reportedly tried but failed to persuade armed factions in Gaza to uphold the truce with Israel.[^cite_note-194] Following escalating rocket fire from Gaza, Israel issued a warning on 4 July that it "would only be able to sustain militant rocket fire for another 24, or maximum 48, hours before undertaking a major military offensive."[^cite_note-195] Hamas declared it was prepared to halt the rocket fire in exchange for an agreement by Israel to stop airstrikes. Netanyahu said Israel would only act against further rocket attacks.[^cite_note-196] On 5 July, Hamas official Osama Hamdan said rocket fire would continue until Israel lifted its import restrictions on Gaza and the Palestinian Authority transferred money to pay Hamas civil servants.[^cite_note-197] Between 4 and 6 July, a total of 62 rockets were fired from Gaza and the IAF attacked several targets in Gaza.[^cite_note-198][^cite_note-199][^cite_note-200] The following day, Hamas assumed formal responsibility for launching rocket attacks on Israel.[^cite_note-nathanthrall-38] Hamas increased rocket attacks on Israel,[^cite_note-economist-two-big-121] and by 7 July had fired 100 rockets from Gaza at Israeli territory; at the same time, the [Israeli Air Force](177619.md) had bombed several sites in Gaza.[^cite_note-201][^cite_note-202][^cite_note-203] Early on 8 July, the IAF bombed 50 targets in the Gaza Strip.[^cite_note-pounds-dozens-204] Israel's military also stopped a militant infiltration from the sea.[^cite_note-pounds-dozens-204] Brigadier General [Moti Almoz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moti_Almoz), the chief spokesman of the Israeli military, said: "We have been instructed by the political echelon to hit Hamas hard."[^cite_note-Goldberg1-118] Hamas insisted that Israel end all attacks on Gaza, release those re-arrested during the crackdown in the West Bank, lift the blockade on Gaza and return to the cease-fire conditions of 2012 as conditions for a ceasefire.[^cite_note-205]

## Operation timeline

Main article: [Timeline of the 2014 Gaza War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Timeline_of_the_2014_Gaza_War)



Israeli residents of [Ashkelon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ashkelon) run for shelter during a rocket alert



IDF-released map of rocket launch sites in Gaza



Israeli troops and tanks near the Gaza border

### Phase 1: Air strikes

As the Israeli operation began, and the IDF bombarded targets in the Gaza Strip with artillery and airstrikes, Hamas continued to fire rockets and mortar shells into Israel in response. A [cease-fire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cease-fire) proposal was announced by the Egyptian government on 14 July, backed by Palestinian president Mahmoud Abbas;[^cite_note-ceasefire_support-206] the Israeli government accepted it and temporarily stopped hostilities on the morning of 15 July, but Hamas rejected it in "its current form", citing the fact Hamas has not been consulted in the formation of the ceasefire and it omitted many of their demands.[^cite_note-pflp-207][^cite_note-208] By 16 July, the death toll within Gaza had surpassed 200 people.[^cite_note-209]

### Phase 2: Ground invasion

On 16 July, Hamas and [Islamic Jihad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic_Jihad_Movement_in_Palestine) offered the Israeli government a 10-year [truce](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Truce) with ten conditions centred on the lifting of the blockade and the release of prisoners who were released in the [Gilad Shalit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gilad_Shalit) prisoner swap and were re-arrested; it was not accepted.[^cite_note-210][^cite_note-211] On 17 July, a five-hour humanitarian [ceasefire](592591.md), proposed by the UN, took place. Approximately five and a half hours prior to the ceasefire's effect, the IDF sighted 13 armed Hamas militants emerging from a Gazan tunnel on the Israeli side of the Gaza border. IDF destroyed the tunnel's exit, ending the incursion.[^cite_note-Elior_Levy-212][^cite_note-John-213] After the ceasefire, IDF began a ground offensive on the Gaza Strip focused on destroying [tunnels crossing the Israel border](43413500.md). On 20 July, the Israeli military entered [Shuja'iyya](20956585.md), a populous neighborhood of [Gaza City](55387.md), resulting in [heavy fighting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Shuja'iyya_\(2014\)).

On 24 July, over 10,000 Palestinians in the West Bank protested against the Israeli operation; 2 Palestinian protesters were killed.[^cite_note-214] 150 Hamas militants who surrendered to the IDF were being questioned about Hamas operations.[^cite_note-auto-215] On 25 July, an Israeli airstrike killed Salah Abu Hassanein, the leader of Islamic Jihad's military wing.[^cite_note-216] On 26 July, another humanitarian ceasefire took place for twelve hours,[^cite_note-217] followed by a unilateral extension by Israel for another twenty-four hours, which was rejected by Hamas.[^cite_note-218] The Palestinian death toll in the Gaza Strip topped 1,000.[^cite_note-219]

On 1 August, the US and UN announced that Israel and Hamas had agreed to a 72-hour ceasefire starting at 08\:00. There was dispute about the terms of the ceasefire: Israel and the US stated that they allowed Israel to "continue to do operations to destroy tunnels that pose a threat to Israeli territory that lead from the Gaza Strip into Israel proper as long as those tunnels exist on the Israel side of their lines"; Hamas said that it would not accept such a condition.[^cite_note-220][^cite_note-BluDeg-221] The ceasefire broke down almost immediately after it started. Israel blamed Hamas for violating the ceasefire, saying a group of Israeli soldiers were attacked by Palestinian militants emerging from a tunnel. Palestinians said the IDF was the first to breach the ceasefire when at 08\:30 it destroyed 19 buildings while undertaking work to demolish tunnels.[^cite_note-BluDeg-221] According to the [PLO](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PLO), the [Palestinian Authority](24093.md) and Gazan sources, Hamas attacked an Israeli unit, killing an Israeli officer (Hadar Goldin, who was initially thought to have been captured) while Israeli forces were still engaged in military activities in [Rafah](584373.md) on Gaza's territory before the truce came into effect. Tweets reported the battle in Rafah before the deadline for the cease-fire.[^cite_note-BluDeg-221] Hamas also killed two soldiers in a [suicide bombing](18835454.md) attack.[^cite_note-222] Senior Hamas leader Moussa Abu Marzouk accused Israel of creating pretexts to undermine the Gaza ceasefire and said that Palestinian fighters abducted the officer and killed the two soldiers before the start of the humanitarian truce,[^cite_note-223] which a Hamas witness has stated began at 7\:30 and lasted five minutes,[^cite_note-224] while Israel said the event took place at 09\:20, after the 08\:00 start of the ceasefire.[^cite_note-225][^cite_note-necessary-226][^cite_note-227]

### Phase 3: Withdrawal of Israeli troops

On 3 August, IDF pulled most of its ground forces out of the Gaza Strip after completing the destruction of 32 tunnels built by Hamas and other militants.[^cite_note-withdrawal2-51][^cite_note-withdrawal-228][^cite_note-229] On 5 August, Israel announced that it had arrested Hossam Kawasmeh on 11 July, and suspected him of having organized the killing of the three teenagers. According to court documents, Kawasmeh stated that Hamas members in Gaza financed the recruitment and arming of the killers.[^cite_note-230][^cite_note-231]

On 10 August, another Egyptian proposal for a 72-hour ceasefire was negotiated and agreed upon Israeli and Palestinian officials, and on 13 August it was extended for another 120 hours to allow both sides to continue negotiations for a long-term solution to end the month-long fighting.[^cite_note-federman1-232] On 19 August, a 24-hour ceasefire extension renewal was violated just hours after agreement with 29 Hamas rockets fired in 20 minutes, with IAF airstrikes in response, killing 9 Gazans. The Israeli delegation was ordered home from Cairo.[^cite_note-cnn.com-233]

On 21 August, an Israeli airstrike in Rafah killed three of Hamas's top commanders: Mohammed Abu Shammala, [Raed al Atar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Raed_al_Atar) and Mohammed Barhoum.[^cite_note-234] During the period from 22 to 26 August, over 700 rockets and mortar shells fired into Israel, killing 3 Israelis. On 26 August, Israel and Hamas accepted another cease-fire at 19\:00.[^cite_note-jpost2-235]

### Result and post-conflict events

On 16 September, a mortar shell was fired to Israel for the first time since the cease-fire commenced. Defense Minister [Moshe Ya'alon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moshe_Ya'alon) reassured border town populations that fighting would not resume with the Gaza Strip at the end of this month, the same time of year as the [Jewish new year](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rosh_Hashanah).[^cite_note-236]

According to Palestinians on 1 October, Israeli forces entered the Gaza Strip and fired upon Palestinian farmers and farms. No injuries were reported.[^cite_note-237][^cite_note-238]

IDF reported that on 31 October a rocket or a mortar shell was launched from Gaza into southern Israel without causing harm.[^cite_note-239]

On 23 November, a Palestinian farmer was shot dead in Gaza, marking the first time a Palestinian from Gaza had been killed by Israeli fire since the seven-week war between Israel and Hamas militants ended with an Egyptian-brokered ceasefire on 26 August. The Israeli army said two Palestinians had approached the border fence and had ignored calls to halt, prompting troops to fire warning shots in the air. "Once they didn't comply, they fired towards their lower extremities. There was one hit," a spokeswoman said.[^cite_note-240]

## Impact

### On Gaza residents



A map showing the location of damage in Gaza up to 25 July for northern Gaza and 1 August for southern Gaza

[^cite_note-241]



Ruins of buildings in [Beit Hanoun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beit_Hanoun), August 2014



Five-year-old Shaymaa al-Masri was injured on 9 July 2014.

[^cite_note-242]

As of 20 July 2014, hospitals in Gaza were ill-equipped and faced severe shortages of various kinds of medicine, medical supplies, and fuel.[^cite_note-243] In response, Israel set up an [IDF field hospital for Gazans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IDF_field_hospital_for_Gazans) at the Erez Crossing and Egypt temporarily reopened the Rafah crossing with Gaza to allow medical supplies to enter and injured Palestinians to receive treatment in Egypt.[^cite_note-244][^cite_note-245] Due to the operation, prices of food, including fish and produce, rose dramatically.[^cite_note-246] A 21 July news report stated that over 83,000 Palestinians had taken shelter in UN facilities.[^cite_note-CNN-crisis-247] Fatah officials accused Hamas of mishandling humanitarian aid meant for civilians. According to them, Hamas took the aid, which included clothing, mattresses, medicine, water, and food, and distributed it among Hamas members or sold it on the black market for profit.[^cite_note-248][^cite_note-hamas-fatah-tensions-249]

According to the [United Nations Office for the Coordination of Humanitarian Affairs](1602284.md) (OCHA), over 273,000 Palestinians in the Gaza Strip had been displaced as of 31 July 2014, of whom 236,375 (over eleven percent of the Gazan population) were taking shelter in 88 UNRWA schools. UNRWA exhausted its capacity to absorb displaced persons, and overcrowding in shelters risked the outbreak of epidemics. 1.8 million people were affected by a halt or reduction of the water supply, 138 schools and 26 health facilities[^cite_note-OCHA1-250][^cite_note-251][^cite_note-252] were damaged, 872 homes were totally destroyed or severely damaged, and the homes of 5,005 families were damaged but still inhabitable. Throughout the Gaza Strip, people received only 2 hours of electricity per day. Power outage had an immediate effect on the public health situation and reduced water and sanitation services, with hospitals becoming dependent on generators. On 2 September, UNRWA reported that 58,217 people were sheltering in 31 of their school buildings, a fifth of their buildings.[^cite_note-253]

OCHA estimated that at least 373,000 children required [psychosocial support](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Psychosocial_support).[^cite_note-OCHA-13] "Intense overcrowding, compounded by the limited access of humanitarian staff to certain areas, is increasingly undermining the living conditions at many shelters and raising protection concerns. Water supply has been particularly challenging..."[^cite_note-254] More than 485,000 [internally displaced persons](427408.md) were in need of emergency food assistance.[^cite_note-OCHA1-250]



The [Gaza Power Plant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaza_Power_Plant) after it was attacked by the Israeli army on 29 July 2014.

Gaza City, home to 500,000, suffered damage to 20–25% of its housing. [Beit Hanoun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beit_Hanoun), with 70% of its housing stock damaged, is considered uninhabitable, with 30,000 residents there in need of accommodation. The only [power station in the Strip](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaza_Power_Plant) was damaged on 29 July, and the infrastructure of power transmission lines and sewage pumps was severely damaged, with a major sewage pipe catering to 500,000 badly damaged. Among the infrastructure targeted and destroyed by Israel's bombing campaign were 220 factories in various industrial zones, including a major carpentry enterprise, construction companies, a major biscuit factory, dairy farms and livestock, a candy manufacturer, the orange groves of Beit Hanoun, Gaza's largest mosques, and several TV stations. Farms, as a consequence of damage or the presence of unexploded ordnance dropped during the conflict, are often inaccessible, and the damage to agriculture was estimated at over \$200 million. 10 out of 26 hospitals closed.[^cite_note-255][^cite_note-theguardian.com-256][^cite_note-make-us-dependent-257]



The mosque in [Khuzaʽa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khuza%CA%BDa,_Khan_Yunis) was amongst those destroyed in the war.

According to the Palestinian Ministry of Endowments and Religious Affairs, 203 mosques were damaged during the war, with 73 being destroyed completely.[^cite_note-Heritage_destroyed-258] Two of Gaza's three Christian churches were also damaged, with the third suffering some damage to peripheral buildings owned by the parish.[^cite_note-Heritage_destroyed-258] In light of damage to mosques, Manuel Musallam informed Muslims they could call their prayers from Christian churches.[^cite_note-259] Some of the mosques were described as "historic"; one was 700 years old and another 500 years old.[^cite_note-260] Israel maintained that Hamas had a routine military use of mosques and that made them legitimate military targets. According to the IDF, 160 rockets were launched from mosques during the war.[^cite_note-261][^cite_note-Church_rockets-262] It also stated that mosques were used for weapon storage, tunnel entrances, training and gathering of militants.[^cite_note-263][^cite_note-264] In one Associated Press report, residents denied that mosques damaged by Israeli forces had been used for military purposes.[^cite_note-265] Cultural heritage sites were also damaged, including [Tell es-Sakan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tell_es-Sakan), [Tell Umm el-'Amr](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tell_Umm_el-'Amr), and the [Byzantine Church of Jabalia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Byzantine_Church_of_Jabalia).[^cite_note-266]

The UN calculated that more than 7,000 homes for 10,000 families were razed, together with an additional 89,000 homes damaged, of which roughly 10,000 were severely affected by the bombing.[^cite_note-ynetnews.com-65] Rebuilding costs were calculated to run from 4–6 billion dollars, over 20 years.[^cite_note-haaretz.com-66]

### On Israeli residents



A kindergarten in central Israel during a rocket attack

[^cite_note-267]



One of the shelter signs that were placed in the [Ben Gurion Airport](113149.md) because of the rocket attacks on Israel

[^cite_note-ynetnumbers-56]

Hamas and other Islamist groups in Gaza fired rockets and mortars at Israeli towns and villages. Despite Israel's use of the Iron Dome missile defense systems, six civilians were killed, including an Arab Israeli and a [Thai](30128.md) civilian worker.[^cite_note-268] An Israeli teen was seriously injured in a rocket strike in the city of Ashkelon.[^cite_note-269] Medical health professionals have noted that Israeli teens prone to mental health problems suffer increasingly during both short-term and long-term conflicts. Experts have identified a number of mental health symptoms which rise during conflict, including anxiety, depression, obsessive-compulsive disorder, interpersonal sensitivity, phobias, and paranoia. There is some doubt whether these issues will dissipate after the conflict is resolved.[^cite_note-270]

Rocket attacks from Gaza caused damage to Israeli civilian infrastructure, including factories, gas stations, and homes.[^cite_note-271]

At the onset of the operation, the Israeli government cancelled all programs within 40 km (25 mi) of Gaza, and requested all people stay at home or near shelter. All summer camps were closed and universities cancelled their final exams.[^cite_note-272] Additionally, all gatherings of 300 or more people were banned.[^cite_note-273] Due to the trajectory of rocket fire from Gaza, many flights in and out of [Ben-Gurion Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ben-Gurion_Airport) were delayed or rerouted.[^cite_note-274] and flights to Ben-Gurion airport [were interrupted for some days](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_Ben_Gurion_Airport_flight_bans) after a Hamas rocket struck an area in its vicinity. Hamas called the FAA flight ban a "great victory".[^cite_note-275] [Michael Ross](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_Ross_\(Mossad_officer\)) wrote that the decision was driven by anxiety and caused considerably more damage than the potential danger it prevented.[^cite_note-276]

About 4,600 claims for direct damage and 28,000 for indirect damage such as missed work days were submitted to Israel's Tax Authority, which paid ₪133 million for direct damage and ₪1.51 billion for indirect damage.[^cite_note-ynetnumbers-56][^cite_note-277][^cite_note-278]

The Bedouin communities in the Negev, living in many habitations built illegally and unrecognised by the Israeli government, were classified as "open areas" and so their 200,000 residents did not have warning sirens or anti-rocket protection.[^cite_note-279]

In Israel, an estimated 5,000[^cite_note-mughrabinidal-280] to 8,000[^cite_note-281] citizens temporarily fled their homes due to the threat of rocketry from Gaza.[^cite_note-mughrabinidal-280] The economic cost of the operation is estimated at NIS 8.5 billion (approximately US\$2.5 billion) and GDP loss of 0.4%.[^cite_note-IsraelLoss-68] At the conclusion of hostilities 3,000–3,700 claims for damages had been submitted by Israelis, and \$41 million paid out for property damage and missed work days.[^cite_note-ynetnumbers-56] Reconstruction costs were estimated at \$11 million.[^cite_note-282]

## Casualties and losses

Most sources agreed that 2,200 Palestinians were killed, 65–70% of them civilians. Israel stated that 72 of its citizens were killed, of whom 67 were soldiers. [+972 magazine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/+972_magazine) pointed out that in this war, just like in other Gaza wars, the casualties were disproportionately Palestinian.[^cite_note-283]

### Palestinian

Further information: [List of Israeli strikes and Palestinian casualties in the 2014 Israel–Gaza conflict](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Israeli_strikes_and_Palestinian_casualties_in_the_2014_Israel%E2%80%93Gaza_conflict)

According to the main estimates between 2,125[^cite_note-MFA-report-fatalities-23] and 2,310[^cite_note-Ma'an2015Jan-19] Gazans were killed and between 10,626[^cite_note-Ma'an2015Jan-19] and 10,895[^cite_note-PCHR-62] were wounded (including 3,374 children, of whom over 1,000 were left permanently disabled[^cite_note-284]). An estimated 64–70% of Palestinians killed were civilians. These estimates were made by the [Gaza Health Ministry](52186562.md), the Israeli organization [B'Tselem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/B'Tselem), two United Nations organs (the [UNHRC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UNHRC) and the [OCHA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/OCHA)) and the [Palestinian Center for Human Rights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian_Center_for_Human_Rights).[^cite_note-OCHA-13][^cite_note-jihad121-20][^cite_note-PCHR-62] Israeli officials estimated that around 40–50% of those killed were militants.[^cite_note-yahooDisputeCasulaties-285][^cite_note-partten-64] On 5 August, OCHA stated that 520,000 Palestinians in the Gaza Strip (approximately 30% of its population) might have been displaced, of whom 485,000 needed emergency food assistance[^cite_note-OCHA1-250] and 273,000 were taking shelter in 90 UN-run schools.[^cite_note-286]

| [OCHA](1602284.md) | 2,220[^cite_note-OCHA20152-293] | 1,492 | 551 | 299 |  | 605 | 123 | 71% | March 2015 |  |
| [Israeli MFA](18133296.md) | 2,125[^cite_note-MFA-report-fatalities2-294] | 761[^cite_note-MFA-report-fatalities2-294] |  |  |  | 936[^cite_note-MFA-report-fatalities2-294] | 428[^cite_note-MFA-report-fatalities2-294] | 36%[^cite_note-MFA-report-fatalities2-294] | 14 June 2015[^cite_note-MFA-report-fatalities2-294] | Uses its own intelligence reports as well as Palestinian sources and media reports to determine combatant deaths.[^cite_note-MFA-report-fatalities2-294][^cite_note-yahooDisputeCasulaties2-295] |
| [Palestinian Centre for Human Rights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian_Centre_for_Human_Rights) | 2,216[^cite_note-AR-25] | 1,543[^cite_note-AR-25] | 556[^cite_note-AR-25] | 293[^cite_note-AR-25] |  | 673 |  | 69% | 3 June 2015 | Includes information collected via interviews with eyewitnesses of the event and family of the victim[^cite_note-AR-25] |

The UN and other organizations classified fatalities as civilians based on eyewitness accounts, interviews with family members and contacts with other field researchers.[^cite_note-yahooDisputeCasulaties-285] Israeli government said its figures were based on "intelligence",[^cite_note-yahooDisputeCasulaties-285] but did not provide any further details or evidence.[^cite_note-nytimes_methodology-296] The Israeli group ITIC provided details for its casualty count, but only for the first 152 casualties (7% of total dead), and stated that 47% killed were militants.[^cite_note-nytimes_methodology-296] The *New York Times* noted that the proportion of deaths that were female rose later in the war.[^cite_note-nytimes_methodology-296] Israeli sources claim that Palestinian militants try to "conceal" their dead; however, a New Zealander source said Palestinian armed groups claim their dead and hold "big funerals" for them, as that [martyrdom is a matter of pride for them](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martyrdom_in_Palestinian_society).[^cite_note-nytimes_methodology-296] Another question revolved around who is a combatant. Israeli sources tend to classify civilian employees of Hamas (such as those employed by various government ministries or political offices) as "terrorists".[^cite_note-nytimes_methodology-296] However, [Human Rights Watch](46530.md) criticizes this practice as contrary to [International Humanitarian Law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Humanitarian_Law) and cautions that civilian employees of Hamas can't be targeted.[^cite_note-nytimes_methodology-296]

The IDF captured the bodies of 19 Hamas fighters killed during the war. Israel continues to hold the bodies pending a prisoner exchange deal.[^cite_note-297]

Human rights groups and the UN use the Gaza Health Ministry's number of Palestinians killed in Gaza as preliminary and add to or subtract from it after conducting their own investigations. For example, human rights groups say that the casualty count provided by the Health Ministry most likely includes victims of [Hamas executions](#Killing_of_suspected_collaborators), domestic violence, and natural deaths,[^cite_note-nytimes_methodology-296] but they (the human rights groups) remove the accused collaborators (who were shot as close range) from their own counts.[^cite_note-executions-298] Israel contends that the Health Ministry's casualty count also includes deaths caused by [rocket or mortar malfunctions](#Killing_and_shooting_of_Gazan_civilians).[^cite_note-nytimes_methodology-296]

According to the OCHA 2015 overview, of the 2,220 Palestinians killed in the conflict, 742 fatalities came from 142 families, who suffered the loss of 3 or more family members in individual bombing incidents on residential buildings.[^cite_note-OCHA2015-299] According to data provided by the Palestinian International Middle East Media Center, 79.7% of the Palestinians killed in Gaza were male, with the majority between 16 and 35. In contrast, a New York Times analysis states that males of ages that are most likely to be militants form 9% of the population but 34% of the casualties, while women and children under 15, who are least likely to be legitimate targets, form 71% of the general population and 33% of the casualties.[^cite_note-nytimes_methodology-296][^cite_note-300] Israel has pointed to the relatively small numbers of fatalities among women, children and men over 60, and to instances of Hamas fighters being counted as civilians (perhaps due to the broad definition of "civilian" used by the Gaza Health Ministry), to support its view that the number of the dead who were militants is 40–50%.[^cite_note-yahooDisputeCasulaties-285] The [IDF](39237.md) calculates that 5% of Gaza's military forces were killed in the war.[^cite_note-heres-what-really-happened-301] Jana Krause, from the war studies department at [King's College London](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/King's_College_London), stated that "a potential explanation other than combatant roles" for the tendency of the dead to be young men "could be that families expect them to be the first ones to leave shelters in order to care for hurt relatives, gather information, look after abandoned family homes or arrange food and water."[^cite_note-BBCdisputeCasualties-302]

Abbas said that "over 850 Hamas members and their families" were killed by Israel during the operation.[^cite_note-303][^cite_note-JPost_Hamas_curfew-304] During the fighting between Israel and Gaza, solidarity protests occurred in the West Bank, during which several Palestinians died; see [Reactions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_Israel%E2%80%93Gaza_conflict#Reactions).

### Israeli

Further information: [List of Palestinian rocket attacks on Israel, 2014](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Palestinian_rocket_attacks_on_Israel,_2014)

A total of 67 IDF soldiers were killed, including one who died of his injuries after two and a half years in a coma, two soldiers – Staff Sergeant [Oron Shaul](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Death_and_ransoming_of_Oron_Shaul) and Second Lieutenant Hadar Goldin – whose bodies are believed to be held in Gaza, and an off-duty soldier who was killed in a rocket attack near his hometown while on leave from service in the war.[^cite_note-305][^cite_note-benari-14][^cite_note-306] Another 469 soldiers were injured.[^cite_note-OPE-Israeli-wounded-17] The IDF said that 5 soldiers were killed and 23 were wounded by [friendly fire](202484.md).[^cite_note-307]

Eleven soldiers were killed, and scores were wounded, in three separate [Hamas tunnel attacks](43413500.md) on IDF units inside Israel. No Israeli civilian casualties were reported in these attacks.

Nine IDF soldiers and six civilians were killed in Israel by Palestinian rocket and mortar fire.[^cite_note-308][^cite_note-309] That means that 64 percent of the victims of Hamas rocket and mortar attacks were IDF soldiers.

According to [Magen David Adom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Magen_David_Adom), 837 civilians were treated for shock (581) or injuries (256): 36 were injured by shrapnel, 33 by debris from shattered glass and building debris, 18 in traffic accidents which occurred when warning sirens sounded, 159 from falling or trauma while on the way to shelters, and 9 in violence in Jerusalem and [Maale Adumim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maale_Adumim).[^cite_note-310][^cite_note-311]

The first Israeli civilian death occurred at the [Erez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Erez) Checkpoint, where a [Chabad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chabad) rabbi was killed by mortar fire. He had come from the West Bank settlement of [Bet Arye](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bet_Arye) to deliver food and drinks to IDF soldiers on the front line.[^cite_note-312][^cite_note-313][^cite_note-314]

The second Israeli civilian to be killed was Awda Lafi al-Waj, a 32-year-old Israeli [Bedouin](61770.md), who was hit by a rocket in the Bedouin village of [Qasr al-Sir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qasr_al-Sir), near [Dimona](9103.md). In spite of having been recognized as a regular settlement in 1999, the village was still not hooked up to the national electricity grid. The rocket alarm system therefore was not operative in the village. The village had still not an approved master plan, which meant that all construction was illegal. There were no shelters, safe rooms or protective walls in the village. The area where the village was located was considered "open area" by military authorities and was therefore not defended by the [Iron Dome](18342427.md) air defense system.[^cite_note-AI-N.Oz-315][^cite_note-316]

A Thai migrant worker was also killed by mortar fire while working at a greenhouse in [moshav](1597952.md) [Netiv HaAsara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Netiv_HaAsara), located a few hundred meters from the Gaza border. The IDF had previously converted the car park in the southern part of the village into an army base and placed tanks there.[^cite_note-317] The moshav provided no protection for the migrant workers working in the fields or greenhouses, located too far from the shelters.[^cite_note-318]

On 22 August, a 4-year-old Israeli child was killed by a mortar that fell on kibbutz [Nahal Oz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nahal_Oz).[^cite_note-AI-N.Oz-315][^cite_note-319]

Two Israeli civilians in kibbutz [Nirim](1965659.md) were killed in a barrage of mortar fire, an hour before a ceasefire went into effect. The two were the settlement's chief and deputy military security co-ordinators.[^cite_note-320] Both Nahal Oz and Nirim (as well as two other nearby settlements) were used as staging areas for IDF tanks operating inside Gaza. The settlements were used for envelopment, surveillance and military supply. [Eyal Weizman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eyal_Weizman) commented that "Israel claims that Hamas is endangering its civilian population by locating its installations in inhabited areas... Israel does the same, though it has enough space to choose not to."[^cite_note-321]

### Economic impact

Palestinian officials estimated on 4 September that, with 17,000 homes destroyed by Israeli bombing, the reconstruction would cost \$7.8 billion, which is about three times Gaza's GDP for 2011.[^cite_note-322][^cite_note-323] Gaza City suffered damage to 20–25% of its housing and [Beit Hanoun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beit_Hanoun) with 70% of its housing uninhabitable.[^cite_note-theguardian.com-256] *[The New York Times](30680.md)* noted that damage in this third war was more severe than in the two preceding wars, where in the aftermath of the earlier [Operation Cast Lead](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Cast_Lead) the damage inflicted was \$4 billion, 3 times the then GDP of Gaza's economy.[^cite_note-NYTGazaEconomy-324] Strikes on Gaza's few industries will take years to repair. Gaza's main power plant on Salaheddin Road was damaged. Two sewage pumping stations in Zeitoun were damaged. The biggest private company in Gaza, the Alawda biscuit and ice cream factory, employing 400, was destroyed by a shelling barrage on 31 July, a few days after undertaking to supply its Choco Sandwich biscuits to 250,000 refugees in response to a request from the [World Food Programme](33582.md); other strikes targeted a plastics factory, a sponge-making plant, the offices of Gaza's main fruit distribution network, the El Majd Industrial and Trading Corporation's factory for cardboard box, carton and plastic bag production, Gaza's biggest dairy product importer and distributor, Roward International. Trond Husby, chief of the UN's Gaza development programme in Gaza, commented that the level of destruction now is worse than in [Somalia](27358.md), [Sierra Leone](27308.md), [South Sudan](32350676.md) and [Uganda](31816.md).[^cite_note-make-us-dependent-257]

A number of tunnels leading into both Israel and Egypt were destroyed throughout the operation. There were reports that the tunnels between Gaza and Egypt were bringing an estimated \$700 million into Gaza's economy through goods or services. Several Palestinians argued that the tunnels had been critical to supporting the residents of Gaza, either through the employment they provided or through the goods that they allowed in—goods which were otherwise not available unless shipped through Egypt.[^cite_note-325] However, tunnels along the Israeli border serve a purely military purpose.[^cite_note-326]

During the ground invasion, Israeli forces destroyed livestock in Gaza. In [Beit Hanoun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beit_Hanoun), 370 cows were killed by tank shelling and airstrikes. In [Beit Lahiya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beit_Lahiya), 20 camels were shot by ground forces.[^cite_note-327] Israel's Minister of Finance estimated that the operation would cost Israel NIS 8.5 billion (approximately US\$2.5 billion), which is similar to [Operation Cast Lead](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Cast_Lead) in 2009 and higher than [Operation Pillar of Defense](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Pillar_of_Defense) in 2012. The forecast included military and non-military costs, including military expenditure and property damage. The calculation indicated that if the operation lasted 20 days, the loss in GDP would be 0.4%.[^cite_note-IsraelLoss-68]

## Reactions

Main article: [International reactions to the 2014 Gaza War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_reactions_to_the_2014_Gaza_War)

### International



[Quds Day 2014](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reactions_to_the_2014_Israel%E2%80%93Gaza_conflict#2014_Quds_Day) pro-Palestinian protest in [Berlin](3354.md), 25 July 2014



[Pro-Israel demonstration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reactions_to_the_2014_Israel%E2%80%93Gaza_conflict#Pro-Israel) in [Helsinki, Finland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Helsinki,_Finland)

International reactions to the conflict came from many [countries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Country) and [international organizations](18201224.md) around the world.

[Canada](5042916.md) was supportive of Israel[^cite_note-328] and critical of Hamas. The [BRICS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BRICS) countries called for restraint on both sides and a return to peace talks based on the [Arab Peace Initiative](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arab_Peace_Initiative). The [European Union](9317.md) condemned the violations of the laws of war by both sides, while stressing the "unsustainable nature of the status quo", and calling for a settlement based on the [two-state solution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Two-state_solution). The [Non-Aligned Movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Non-Aligned_Movement), the [Arab League](52625.md), and most Latin American countries were critical of Israel, with some countries in the latter group withdrawing their ambassadors from Israel in protest. South Africa called for restraint by both sides and an end to "collective punishment of Palestinians".

There were many pro-Israel and pro-Palestine demonstrations worldwide, including inside Israel and the Palestinian territories. According to OCHA, 23 Palestinians were killed and 2,218 were wounded by the IDF (38% of the latter by live fire) during these demonstrations.[^cite_note-Ma'an_News-329][^cite_note-The_IDFs_real_face-330][^cite_note-OCHA-West-Bank-331]

### United States

[U.S. President](24113.md) [Barack Obama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barack_Obama) acknowledged Israel's right to defend itself, but urged restraint by both sides. Meanwhile, the [United States Congress](31756.md) expressed vigorous support for Israel. It passed legislation providing Israel with an additional \$225 million in military aid for missile defense with a bipartisan 395–8 vote in the [House of Representatives](19468510.md) and by unanimous consent in the [Senate](24909346.md).[^cite_note-332] This was in addition to strong measures supporting Israel's position passed with overwhelming support in both houses.[^cite_note-333] Israel received strong statements of bipartisan support from the leadership and members of both houses of Congress for its actions during the conflict. A poll conducted in July found that 57% of Americans believed the operation was justified.[^cite_note-334]

During the [U.S. presidential primary race of 2016](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_Democratic_Party_presidential_primaries), Democratic candidate [Bernie Sanders](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bernie_Sanders) criticized Israel for its treatment of Gaza, and in particular criticized Netanyahu for "overreacting" and causing unnecessary civilian deaths.[^cite_note-335] In April 2016, the [Anti-Defamation League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-Defamation_League) called on Sanders to withdraw remarks he made to the *[Daily News](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daily_News_\(New_York\))*, which the ADL said exaggerated the death toll of the 2014 Israel–Gaza conflict. Sanders said "over 10,000 innocent people were killed", a number far in excess of Palestinian or Israel sources' estimates.[^cite_note-336] In response, Sanders said that he accepted a corrected number of the death toll as 2,300 during the course of the interview, which was taped, and that he would make every effort to set the record straight. The written transcript of the interview failed to note that Sanders said "Okay" to the corrected number presented by the interviewer during the course of the interview.[^cite_note-337]

### Gaza

On 6 August 2014, thousands of Palestinians rallied in Gaza in support of [Hamas](13913.md), demanding an end to the blockade of Gaza.[^cite_note-338] After the 26 August ceasefire, the [Palestinian Center for Policy and Survey Research](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian_Center_for_Policy_and_Survey_Research) conducted a poll in the West Bank and the Gaza Strip: 79% of respondents said that Hamas had won the war and 61% said that they would pick Hamas leader [Ismail Haniyeh](41207463.md) as the Palestinian president, up from 41% before the war.[^cite_note-339]

According to *[The Washington Post](102226.md)*, a percentage of Gazans held Hamas accountable for the humanitarian crisis and wanted the militants to stop firing rockets from their neighborhoods to avoid Israeli reaction.[^cite_note-340] Some of the Gazans have attempted to protest against Hamas, which routinely accuses protesters of being Israeli spies and has killed more than 50 such protesters.[^cite_note-341][^cite_note-342] Around 6 August, Palestinian protesters reportedly attacked and beat up Hamas spokesperson Sami Abu Zuhri because they blamed Hamas for inciting Operation Protective Edge.[^cite_note-343][^cite_note-344]

An unknown number of Palestinians, estimated in the hundreds or thousands, tried to flee to Europe due to the conflict. The Palestinian rights group Adamir collected the names of 400 missing persons. In what was described by [International Organization for Migration](36893.md) as the "worst shipwreck in years", a boat carrying refugees was rammed by smugglers and capsized off the coast of [Malta](19137.md), resulting in the deaths of about 400 people. According to interviews with survivors, they paid smugglers between \$2,000–\$5,000 or used legal travel permits, to get to Egypt. One refugee who died had considered the boat to be rickety but told his father "I have no life in Gaza anyway".[^cite_note-345][^cite_note-346]

### Israel and the West Bank



A pro-Israeli demonstration supporting Israel and the [Israel Defense Forces](39237.md) in Tzuk Eytan



Banner on a kindergarten in [Kiryat Ono](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kiryat_Ono) saying "Dear soldiers! Take care of yourselves! You are our heroes!"



Demonstration against Operation Protective Edge in [Tel Aviv](31453.md), Israel

A majority of the Israeli public supported Operation Protective Edge. A poll conducted after a temporary ceasefire came into effect during the war in July found that 86.5% of Israelis polled opposed the ceasefire.[^cite_note-347] Another poll in July found 91% support for the operation among the Jewish public, with 85% opposed to stopping the war and 51% in support of continuing the war until Hamas was removed from power in Gaza, while 4% believed the war to be a mistake.[^cite_note-348] Two other polls found 90% and 95% support for the war among the Jewish public.[^cite_note-349][^cite_note-350][^cite_note-351] Towards the end of the war, after Israel announced the withdrawal of ground forces from Gaza, a poll found 92% support for the war among the Jewish public, and that 48% believed that the IDF had used the appropriate amount of firepower in the operation, while 45% believed it had used too little and 6% believed it had used too much. The poll also found that 62% of [Israeli-Arabs](492331.md) believed the operation was unjustified while 24% believed it was justified, and that 62% believed too much firepower was used, 10% believed too little firepower was used, and 3% thought the appropriate amount of firepower was used.[^cite_note-352]

The war saw strained relations between Israeli Jews and Israeli Arabs. Many Arab businesses closed as part of a one-day general strike in solidarity with Gaza, leading to Israeli Foreign Minister [Avigdor Lieberman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Avigdor_Lieberman) to call for the boycott of Arab businesses that participated in the strike. Thousands demonstrated against the war, including some who threw stones and blocked streets. About 1,500 Arabs were arrested over involvement in protests against the war. Numerous Arabs were fired or disciplined by their employers over comments against Israel and the war on social media. The most notable case was that of a psychological counselor who worked for the [Lod](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lod) municipality, who was terminated on the orders of the Mayor of Lod after writing a Facebook post expressing joy over the deaths of 13 Israeli soldiers in the [Battle of Shuja'iyya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Shuja'iyya_\(2014\)). Arabs reported an increase in racism and violence from right-wing Jews. However, some Israeli Jews against the war joined in anti-war protests, and a handful were also arrested.[^cite_note-353][^cite_note-354][^cite_note-355][^cite_note-356][^cite_note-357]

There were continuous protests and clashes in the West Bank. The [funeral](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mohammed_Abu_Khdeir#Family_reactions,_riots_and_funeral) of [Mohammed Abu Khdeir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mohammed_Abu_Khdeir) on 4 July was joined by thousands of mourners, and was accompanied by clashes across east Jerusalem throughout the weekend.[^cite_note-358] According to OCHA, 23 Palestinians were killed and 2,218 were wounded by the IDF, 38% of the latter by live fire.[^cite_note-Ma'an_News-329][^cite_note-The_IDFs_real_face-330][^cite_note-OCHA-West-Bank-331] According to the PLO, 32 Palestinians were killed in the West Bank in the period 13 June – 26 August, nearly 1400 were wounded by Israeli fire and 1,700 were detained in the largest offensive in the West Bank since the [Second Intifada](71717.md). The PLO also stated that 1,472 settlement homes had been approved over the summer.[^cite_note-359]

During the war there were over 360 attacks on Jews from the West Bank, a spate that was thought by *[The Jerusalem Post](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Jerusalem_Post)* to have "peaked" on 4 August with a [tractor attack in Jerusalem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_Jerusalem_tractor_attack) and the shooting of a uniformed soldier in the [French Hill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_Hill_\(neighborhood\)) neighborhood, leading to an increase in security in the city.[^cite_note-360][^cite_note-361]

On 1 September, Israel announced a plan to expropriate 1,000 acres of land in the West Bank, reportedly as a "reaction to the deplorable murder in June of three Israeli teenagers", which Amnesty International denounced as the "largest land grab in the Occupied Palestinian Territories since the 1980s".[^cite_note-362][^cite_note-363] The EU complained about the land expropriation and warned of renewed violence in Gaza; the US called it "counterproductive".[^cite_note-364][^cite_note-365]

## Alleged violations of international humanitarian law

Further information: [International law and the Arab–Israeli conflict](1368578.md)

A number of legal and moral issues concerning the conflict arose during course of the fighting.[^cite_note-366] Various human rights groups have argued that both Palestinian rocket attacks and Israeli targeted [destruction of homes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/House_demolition_in_the_Israeli%E2%80%93Palestinian_conflict) of Hamas and other militia members violated international humanitarian law and might constitute war crimes, violations of [international humanitarian law](1093604.md).[^cite_note-btselem1-367][^cite_note-indiscriminate-368][^cite_note-washingtonpost1-369] [Navi Pillay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Navi_Pillay), the [United Nations High Commissioner for Human Rights](22463.md), accused Hamas militants of violating international humanitarian law by "locating rockets within schools and hospitals, or even launching these rockets from densely populated areas."[^cite_note-AP-warcrimes-370] She also criticized Israel's military operation, stating that there was "a strong possibility that international law has been violated, in a manner that could amount to war crimes", and specifically criticizing Israel's actions in Gaza as disproportionate.[^cite_note-371]

Amnesty International found evidence that "\[d]uring the current hostilities, Hamas spokespeople reportedly urged residents in some areas of the Gaza Strip not to leave their homes after the Israeli military dropped leaflets and made phone calls warning people in the area to evacuate", and that international humanitarian law was clear in that "even if officials or fighters from Hamas or Palestinian armed groups associated with other factions did in fact direct civilians to remain in a specific location in order to shield military objectives from attacks, all of Israel's obligations to protect these civilians would still apply."[^cite_note-amnesty1-372] [B'tselem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/B'tselem) found that Hamas had breached provisions of [International Humanitarian Law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Humanitarian_Law) (IHL), both firing from civilian areas and firing at Israeli civilian areas. It also stated that the Israeli policy of bombing homes, formulated by government officials and the senior military command, though claimed to be in [conformity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conformity) with IHL, was 'unlawful', and designed to 'block, a priori, any allegations that Israel breached IHL provisions', in that it relies on an interpretation that leaves 'no restrictions whatsoever on Israeli action' so that 'whatever method it chooses to respond to Hamas operations is legitimate, no matter how horrifying the consequences.'[^cite_note-B'tselemJan2015-373][^cite_note-374]

Hamas leader [Ismail Haniyeh](41207463.md) urged the Palestinian Authority to sign the Rome Statute of the [International Criminal Court](14880.md) (ICC).[^cite_note-375] ICC prosecutor Geoffrey Nice said that a "decision to do nothing clearly emerges from the meeting" with the PA foreign minister [Riad Malki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Riad_Malki).[^cite_note-376][^cite_note-377]

Israel announced its own investigations of both military and civilian leadership and the conduct during the war.[^cite_note-NYTIsraelInvestigation-378] Human rights organizations have expressed little confidence in Israel's measures, citing past experience.[^cite_note-379] Moreover, several human rights organizations were denied access to Gaza by Israel, rendering it impossible for them to carry out on-site investigations.[^cite_note-380][^cite_note-381] [B'Tselem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/B'Tselem) has refused to participate in the army investigation calling it a "whitewashing mechanism". According to *The New York Times*, "Of 44 cases initially referred to army fact-finding teams for preliminary examination, seven have been closed, including one involving the death of eight members of a family when their home was struck on 8 July, the first day of the Israeli air campaign, and others are pending."[^cite_note-NYTIsraelInvestigation-378]

### UN report

The [United Nations Human Rights Council](635790.md) (UNHRC) commissioned the [United Nations Fact Finding Mission on the 2014 Israel–Gaza conflict](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Fact_Finding_Mission_on_the_2014_Israel%E2%80%93Gaza_conflict), led by [William Schabas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/William_Schabas) to investigate war crimes allegations by both sides. Israel criticized Schabas as biased because he had made statements critical of Israel and sympathetic to Hamas. Schabas denied any bias,[^cite_note-382][^cite_note-383] but on 2 February 2015 resigned from the position.[^cite_note-384]

Israel refused to cooperate with the commission by denying them access to the [Occupied Palestinian territories](241405.md) and not responding to a list of questions relating to specific incidents. The [State of Palestine](241405.md) fully cooperated with the investigation.[^cite_note-UNreport-385]

The commission issued its report on 22 June 2015 and found evidence of war crimes by both Israel and Hamas.The report criticized Israel for its disproportionate airstrikes that damaged approximately 18,000 dwellings, its indiscriminate use of weapons in civilian areas, the high number of civilian casualties in the [Battle of Shuja'iyya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Shuja'iyya_\(2014\)) and "[Black Friday](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Black_Friday_\(bombing_campaign\))" attacks on Rafah, and for letting violations of international law by IDF soldiers go unpunished.
