The report also found that rockets fired from Gaza into Israel by Hamas and other Palestinian militants were intended to spread terror.[^cite_note-bbc_UNreport-386][^cite_note-387]

Israel dismissed the investigation as "politically motivated and morally flawed", while [Hamas](13913.md) said it wrongly equated "the victim and executioner".[^cite_note-bbc_UNreport-386]

### Alleged violations by Hamas

#### Killing and shooting of Gazan civilians

&#x20;Twenty alleged spies for Israel from [Shuja'iyya](20956585.md) were killed.[^cite_note-388] A few days later, Hamas reportedly killed two Gazans and wounded ten after a scuffle broke out over food handouts.[^cite_note-389]

The IDF stated on 31 July that more than 280 Hamas rockets[^cite_note-failing_rockets-55] malfunctioned and fell inside the Gaza strip, hitting sites including Al-Shifa Hospital and the Al-Shati refugee camp, killing at least 11 and wounding dozens.[^cite_note-Shifa-10-390] Hamas denied that any of its rockets hit the Gaza Strip.,[^cite_note-failing_rockets-55][^cite_note-391][^cite_note-392] but Palestinian sources said numerous rocket launches ended up falling in Gaza communities and that scores of people have been killed or injured. Israeli Military sources said the failed Hamas launches increased amid heavy Israeli air and artillery strikes throughout the Gaza Strip. They said the failed launches reflected poorly assembled rockets as well as the rush to load and fire projectiles before they are spotted by Israeli aircraft.[^cite_note-393] While the Al-Shifa Hospital incident is disputed, early news reports have suggested that the strike was from an Israeli drone missile.[^cite_note-Shifa-10-390][^cite_note-394][^cite_note-395] Amnesty International concluded that the explosion at the Shati refugee camp on 28 July in which 13 civilians were killed was caused by a Palestinian rocket, despite Palestinian claims it was an Israeli missile.[^cite_note-396]

#### Killing of suspected collaborators

&#x20;During the conflict, Hamas executed Gazan civilians it accused of having collaborated with Israel, thirty on 30 July.[^cite_note-397] The United Nations Investigatory Commission concluded that at least 21 persons[^cite_note-398] were killed in Gaza City in [summary executions](1227309.md) for collaboration between 5 and 22 August 2014 in Gaza City, most on the last date. 11 of those shot by firing squads on 22 August, a day after 3 al-Qassam brigade commanders had been killed in an Israeli strike at Rafah, had been taken from Al-Katiba prison. 7 were shot the same evening in "Operation Strangling Necks" directed against alleged collaborators.[^cite_note-399] [Norman Finkelstein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Norman_Finkelstein) compared the dilemma facing Hamas regarding collaboration within the ranks as similar to that expressed by Jewish leaders in the aftermath of the [Warsaw Ghetto Uprising](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Warsaw_Ghetto_Uprising).[^cite_note-400]

Abbas' Secretary-General, Al-Tayyib Abd al-Rahim, condemned the "random executions of those who Hamas called collaborators", adding that some of those killed had been detained for more than three years.[^cite_note-401][^cite_note-402] Amnesty International, Human Rights Watch and Palestinian human rights groups condemned the executions.[^cite_note-403][^cite_note-404][^cite_note-405] Bodies of the victims were brought to hospitals to be added to the number of civilian casualties of Israeli operation.[^cite_note-executions-298] According to a Shin Bet official, "not even one" of the alleged collaborators executed by Hamas provided any intelligence to Israel, while the Shin Bet officially "confirmed that those executed during Operation Protective Edge had all been held in prison in Gaza in the course of the hostilities."[^cite_note-406]

Senior Hamas official Moussa Abu Marzouk confirmed that some victims were kept under arrest before the conflict began and were executed to satisfy the public without due legal procedure.[^cite_note-fox_executions-407]

[Shurat HaDin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shurat_HaDin) filed a suit with the [ICC](14880.md) charging [Khaled Mashal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khaled_Mashal) with war crimes for the executions of 38 civilians.[^cite_note-fox_executions-407][^cite_note-408] Hamas co-founder [Ayman Taha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ayman_Taha) was found dead; *[Al-Quds Al-Arabi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Quds_Al-Arabi)* reported he had been shot by Hamas for maintaining contact with the intelligence services of several Arab countries; Hamas stated he was targeted by an Israeli airstrike.

On 26 May 2015, Amnesty International released a report saying that Hamas carried out extrajudicial killings, abductions and arrests of Palestinians and used the [Al-Shifa Hospital](21218880.md) to detain, interrogate and torture suspects. It details the executions of at least 23 Palestinians accused of collaborating with Israel and torture of dozens of others, many victims of torture were members of the rival Palestinian movement, Fatah.[^cite_note-409][^cite_note-410]

#### Endangerment of Civilians

##### Human shields

Main article: [Usage of human shields by Hamas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Usage_of_human_shields_by_Hamas)

The [European Union](9317.md) condemned Hamas, and in particular condemned "calls on the civilian population of Gaza to provide themselves as human shields."[^cite_note-411][^cite_note-412] Confirmation of this practice was produced by correspondents from [France24](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/France24), *[The Financial Times](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Financial_Times)*, and [RT](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/RT_\(TV_network\)), who respectively filmed a rocket launch pad which was placed in a civilian area next to a hotel where international journalists were staying,[^cite_note-413] reported on rockets being fired from near Al-Shifa Hospital, and reported on Hamas firing rockets near a hotel.[^cite_note-Timesintimidation-414] In September 2014, a Hamas official acknowledged to an [Associated Press](18935732.md) reporter that the group had fired rockets from civilian areas.[^cite_note-415]

While the Israeli government repeatedly stated that many civilian casualties were the result of Hamas using the Gazan population as [human shields](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_shield)[^cite_note-416] several British media organizations (including *[The Guardian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Guardian)*, and *[The Independent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Independent)*) dismissed such claims as "myths"[^cite_note-417][^cite_note-418] and the [BBC](19344654.md)'s Middle East editor Jeremy Bowen likewise said he "saw no evidence of Hamas using Palestinians as human shields."[^cite_note-419] Additionally the London-based NGO, [Amnesty International](18947898.md), dismissed such claims, stated it was unable to verify them and emphasized that even if they were true the IDF would still have a responsibility to protect civilians.[^cite_note-AMINT-59][^cite_note-420]

The statements fall into two categories: using civilian structures like homes, mosques and hospitals to store munitions in or launch rockets from,[^cite_note-Harkov-human-shields-421] and urging or forcing civilian population to stay in their homes, to shield militants. Israeli soldiers have also said Hamas operatives directly employed women and children as involuntary human shields to evade pursuit,[^cite_note-422][^cite_note-423] while Hamas and others have said such accusations are false.[^cite_note-guardianHamasShield-424] [Asa Kasher](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asa_Kasher), who helped to write the Israel Defense Forces's Code of Conduct, argued that "Israel cannot forfeit its ability to protect its citizens against attacks simply because terrorists hide behind non-combatants. If it did so, it would be giving up any right to self-defense."[^cite_note-Kasher-425]

##### Use of civilian structures for military purposes

The UN High Commissioner for Human Rights (UNHCHR) [Navi Pillay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Navi_Pillay) accused Hamas militants of violating international humanitarian law by "locating rockets within schools and hospitals, or even launching these rockets from densely populated areas." But she added that this did not absolve Israel from disregarding the same law.[^cite_note-AP-warcrimes-370] The UNHCHR report recognised that "the obligation to avoid locating military objectives within densely populated areas is not absolute. The small size of Gaza and its population density make it difficult for armed groups to always comply with this requirement."[^cite_note-426]

In a 2015 report,[^cite_note-427] Amnesty International states that "There are credible reports that, in certain cases, Palestinian armed groups launched rockets or mortars from within civilian facilities or compounds, including schools, at least one hospital and a Greek Orthodox church in Gaza City. In at least two cases, accounts indicate that attacks were launched in spite of the fact that displaced Gazan civilians were sheltering in the compounds or in neighbouring buildings.".

Israel has stated that many mosques, schools and hospitals were used to store weapons. The IDF spokesman said that mortar shells were fired from a boys' school that served as a shelter for refugees.[^cite_note-428] There were reports of the use of mosques to store weapons,[^cite_note-429] and having launch sites very close to civilian structures.[^cite_note-Harkov-human-shields-421] Gaza's Greek Orthodox archbishop has said that Hamas used the church compound, which sheltered 2000 Muslim civilians, to launch rockets into Israel.[^cite_note-Church_rockets-262][^cite_note-430] [France 24](1263244.md) correspondent Gallagher Fenwick reported that a Hamas rocket-launching pad was placed in densely populated neighborhood of Gaza City, about 50 meters from the hotel where the majority of international media were staying and 100 meters from a UN building. Fenwick said that "children can be seen playing on and near the rocket launcher".[^cite_note-431]

Israel released footage of Palestinian militants launching rockets from a school and a cemetery.[^cite_note-432] In at least one case a cemetery was targeted by an Israeli airstrike.[^cite_note-433]

According to [Shabak](311752.md), the Israeli internal security service, some militants, when interrogated, admitted using civilian buildings for military purposes. The admissions included more than ten mosques that were used for gatherings, training, storage of weapons, tunnel activities and military observations. During interrogations, one militant said that he was instructed in case of successful abduction using a tunnel to take the victim to a kindergarten located near its opening.[^cite_note-ITIC_2_sep-434][^cite_note-Haaretz_interrogation-435]



Explosives were allegedly two steps away from a baby's bed in Gaza during the war.

On 24 August, Israel released part of what it says is a Hamas training manual on urban warfare, which states "the process of hiding ammunition inside buildings is intended for ambushes in residential areas and to move the campaign from open areas into built up and closed areas" and "residents of the area should be used to bring in the equipment...take advantage of this to avoid \[Israeli] spy planes and attack drones." The manual also explains how fighting from within civilian population makes IDF operations difficult and what the benefits of civilian deaths are.[^cite_note-436] Hamas spokesman Sami Abu Zuhri dismissed the document as a "forgery...aimed at justifying the mass killings of Palestinian civilians."[^cite_note-Training_Manual-437]

On 12 September, Ghazi Hamad, a senior Hamas official, acknowledged for the first time that Hamas did fire rockets from civilian areas and said "some mistakes were made".[^cite_note-438]

###### In Israel

See also: [Israeli war crimes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_war_crimes)

Israeli and Jewish critics of the war, including [Uri Avnery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Uri_Avnery) and [Gideon Levy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gideon_Levy) wrote that in their own [war of independence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1948_Palestine_war) in 1948 (and earlier), Jews hid weapons in synagogues, kindergartens and schools as well.[^cite_note-439][^cite_note-440] Other critics have noted that the headquarters of the IDF and Shin Bet, as well as an Israeli military training facility, are also located near civilian centers.[^cite_note-guardianHamasShield-424][^cite_note-441] Commentators brought up the current high population density of Gaza in conjunction with Palestinian military activities and installations being in or near civilian structures.[^cite_note-guardianHamasShield-424][^cite_note-channel1-442]

###### Legality

Using civilian structures to store munitions and launch attacks from is unlawful, and the [Fourth Geneva Convention](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fourth_Geneva_Convention) states that "The presence of a protected person may not be used to render certain points or areas immune from military operations."[^cite_note-443] On the other hand, another convention says that "Any violation of these prohibitions shall not release the parties to the conflict from their legal obligations with respect to the civilian population and civilians, including the obligation to take the precautionary measures."[^cite_note-444]

According to Harriet Sherwood, writer for the Guardian, even if Hamas were violating the law on this matter, it would not legally justify Israel's bombing of areas where civilians are known to be.[^cite_note-guardianHamasShield-424] Amnesty International stated that "Indiscriminate and disproportionate attacks (where the likely number of civilian casualties or damage to civilian property outweighs the anticipated military advantage to be gained) are ... prohibited."[^cite_note-amnesty1-372] It said that "Israel's relentless air assault on Gaza has seen its forces flagrantly disregard civilian life and property".[^cite_note-445] [Human Rights Watch](46530.md) has said that in many cases "the Israeli military has presented no information to show that it was attacking lawful military objectives or acted to minimize civilian casualties."[^cite_note-hrwUnlawfulAistrikes-446] An investigation by Human Rights Watch found that "in most of the sites we investigated so far (in this conflict) we found no valid military targets".[^cite_note-usatoday.com-447] A high-level group of former diplomats and military experts concluded that "the IDF acted within the bounds of international law during the war."[^cite_note-448] The Israeli government issued a report saying that its military actions were "lawful and legitimate" and that "Israel made substantial efforts to avoid civilian deaths."[^cite_note-449] The High Level Military Group, composed of military experts from Australia, Colombia, France, Germany, India, Italy, Britain, the United States, and Spain, released an assessment on Operation Protective Edge acknowledging Israel made "unprecedented efforts" to avoid civilian casualties exceeding international standards.[^cite_note-450]

##### Medical facilities and personnel



Photo taken during the 72-hour ceasefire between Hamas and Israel on 6 August 2014. A destroyed ambulance in Shuja'iyya in the Gaza Strip.

Medical units including hospitals and medical personnel have special protections under [international humanitarian law](1093604.md). They lose their protection only if they commit, outside their humanitarian function, "acts harmful to the enemy."[^cite_note-451] More than 25 medical facilities were damaged in the conflict; one attack on Al-Aqsa hospital killed 5 people.[^cite_note-452] In many cases, ambulances and other medical personnel were hit.[^cite_note-453] Amnesty International has condemned the attacks and said that there is "mounting evidence" that Israel deliberately targeted hospitals and medical personnel; Israel said it had not.[^cite_note-454]

A [Finnish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Finnish_people) reporter from *[Helsingin Sanomat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Helsingin_Sanomat)* reported seeing rockets fired from near the Gaza Al-Shifa hospital.[^cite_note-455] The IDF said that in several cases Hamas used Wafa hospital as a military base and used ambulances to transport its fighters.[^cite_note-456][^cite_note-457] According to the Israeli [Shabak](311752.md), many of the militants it interrogated said that "everyone knew" that Hamas leaders were using hospitals for hiding. Hamas security reportedly wore police uniforms and blocked access to certain parts of the hospitals. One of the interrogated militants reportedly said that civilians seeking medical attention usually were thrown out by the security.[^cite_note-ITIC_2_sep-434][^cite_note-Haaretz_interrogation-435] The Washington Post described Al-Shifa hospital as a "de facto headquarters for Hamas leaders, who can be seen in the hallways and offices."[^cite_note-458] Amnesty International reported that: "Hamas forces used the abandoned areas of al-Shifa hospital in Gaza City, including the outpatients' clinic area, to detain, interrogate, torture and otherwise ill-treat suspects, even as other parts of the hospital continued to function as a medical centre".[^cite_note-459]

French-Palestinian journalist Radjaa Abu Dagga[^cite_note-460] reported that Hamas militants interrogated him in Gaza's main hospital (Al-Shifa); his report was later removed from his paper's website at his request.[^cite_note-Timesintimidation-414]

Mohammed Al Falahi, Secretary General of [Red Crescent, UAE](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Red_Crescent_Society_of_the_United_Arab_Emirates) said that Hamas militants fired on Israeli planes from Red Crescent's field hospital in order to provoke retaliation, attacked Red Crescent team on their way back and planted land mines on their path.[^cite_note-461][^cite_note-462]

##### Urging or forcing civilians to stay in their homes

The IDF has released photographs which it says show civilians on rooftops, and a video of Hamas spokesperson [Sami Abu Zuhri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sami_Abu_Zuhri) saying "the fact that people are willing to sacrifice themselves against Israeli warplanes in order to protect their homes \[...] is proving itself".[^cite_note-channel1-442][^cite_note-usatoday.com-447][^cite_note-463][^cite_note-464] The EU has strongly condemned "calls on the civilian population of Gaza to provide themselves as human shields"[^cite_note-465][^cite_note-466] and US Congress-members introduced bills condemning Hamas for using human shields.[^cite_note-467][^cite_note-468] Civilians and activists in Gaza used themselves as '[human shields](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_shields)' in attempts to prevent Israeli attacks.[^cite_note-469][^cite_note-470][^cite_note-471][^cite_note-472]

Hamas officials said human shields were not used. One Gazan stated that "nobody is safe and nobody can flee anywhere because everywhere is targeted."[^cite_note-CNN-crisis-247] Many reporters, including from the BBC,[^cite_note-473] the Independent[^cite_note-474] and the Guardian[^cite_note-guardianHamasShield-424] said that they found no evidence of Hamas forcing Palestinians to stay and become unwilling human shields.

Fatah officials said that Hamas placed over 250 Fatah members under house arrest or in jail, putting them under threat of being killed by Israeli strikes and shooting them in the legs or breaking their limbs if they tried to leave.[^cite_note-hamas-fatah-tensions-249][^cite_note-475][^cite_note-476] According to Abbas, more than 300 Fatah members were placed under house arrest and 120 were executed for fleeing.[^cite_note-JPost_Hamas_curfew-304]

[Amnesty International](18947898.md) reported that it did "not have evidence at this point" that Palestinian civilians were intentionally used by Hamas or Palestinian armed groups during the current hostilities to "shield" specific locations or military personnel or equipment from Israeli attacks". It additionally said that "public statements referring to entire areas are not the same as directing specific civilians to remain in their homes as "human shields" for fighters, munitions, or military equipment" and that "even if officials or fighters from Hamas or Palestinian armed groups ... did in fact direct civilians to remain in a specific location in order to shield military objectives ..., all of Israel's obligations to protect these civilians would still apply."[^cite_note-amnesty1-372] Human Rights Watch said many of the attacks on targets appeared to be "disproportionate" and "indiscriminate".[^cite_note-hrwUnlawfulAistrikes-446]

[Human Rights Watch](46530.md) attributed many civilian deaths to the lack of safe places to flee to, and accused Israel of firing at fleeing civilians. It stated that there are many reasons that prevent civilians from abiding by warnings, and that the failure to abide by warnings does not make civilians lawful targets.

#### Rocket attacks on Israeli civilians



House destroyed by a rocket in [Yehud, Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yehud,_Israel)

Human rights organizations, including Amnesty International, pointed to Hamas's rocket attacks on Israeli cities as violations of international law and war crimes.[^cite_note-indiscriminate-368] Palestinian ambassador to the UN Human Rights Council, Ibrahim Khraishi stated in a 9 July interview on PA TV that the "[missiles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qassam_rocket) that are now being launched against Israel – each and every missile constitutes a crime against humanity, whether it hits or misses, because it is directed at civilian targets".[^cite_note-477]

Hamas political figure [Khaled Mashal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khaled_Mashal) has defended the firing of rockets into Israel, saying that "our victims are civilians and theirs are soldiers".[^cite_note-478] According to one report, "nearly all the 2,500–3,000 rockets and mortars Hamas has fired at Israel since the start of the war seem to have been aimed at towns", including an attack on "a kibbutz collective farm close to the Gaza border", in which an Israeli child was killed.[^cite_note-Training_Manual-437] Former Israeli Lt. Col. Jonathan D. Halevi stated that "Hamas has expressed pride in aiming long-range rockets at strategic targets in Israel including the nuclear reactor in Dimona, the chemical plants in Haifa, and Ben-Gurion Airport", which "could have caused thousands" of Israeli casualties "if successful".[^cite_note-479]

According to Israel, Hamas continued to fire rockets at the Erez border crossing while sick and wounded Gazans tried to enter Israel for treatment. The Erez border crossing is the only legal border crossing between Gaza and Israel. Other people affected by this included journalists, UN workers, and volunteers.[^cite_note-480][^cite_note-481]

#### Military use of UN facilities

The [United Nations Relief and Works Agency for Palestine Refugees in the Near East](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Relief_and_Works_Agency_for_Palestine_Refugees_in_the_Near_East) (UNRWA) has a number of institutions and schools in the Gaza region, and, as of 24 July 23 had been closed. Hamas took advantage of the closures to employ some of these vacant UNRWA buildings as weapon storage sites.[^cite_note-FP-482] UNRWA officials, on discovering that three[^cite_note-483][^cite_note-484] such vacated schools had been employed for storing rockets, condemned Hamas's actions, calling it a "flagrant violation of the neutrality of our premises."[^cite_note-485][^cite_note-486][^cite_note-487]

On 16 July,[^cite_note-488] 22 July,[^cite_note-489] and on 29 July, the UNRWA announced that rockets had been found in their schools.[^cite_note-490] Israel's foreign minister [Avigdor Lieberman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Avigdor_Lieberman) stated that UNRWA had turned over some discovered rockets to Hamas.[^cite_note-491] [Israel Democracy Institute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel_Democracy_Institute) Vice President, Mordechai Kremnitzer, accused the UNRWA of [war crimes](48626.md) for handing over the rockets, while [Hebrew University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hebrew_University) Professor Robbie Sabel stated that the UNRWA "had no legal obligation to hand the rockets over to Israel" and had little other choice in the matter.[^cite_note-492] UNRWA states the armouries had been transferred to local police authorities under the Ramallah national unity government's authority, in accordance with "longstanding UN practice in UN humanitarian operations worldwide".[^cite_note-FP-482][^cite_note-493][^cite_note-494] UN Secretary [Ban Ki-moon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ban_Ki-moon) ordered an investigation.[^cite_note-495]

On 30 July, the IDF said that they had discovered the entrance to a [tunnel](43413500.md) concealed inside a UNRWA medical clinic in [Khan Yunis](101330.md). The clinic was rigged with explosives, which then exploded and killed three Israeli soldiers.[^cite_note-UNWRAbomb-496] This report was later corrected by the Coordinator of Government Activities in the Territories, the military unit that implements government policies in the Palestinian areas, who later that day stated that despite its UNRWA sign, the site was not registered as belonging to UNRWA.[^cite_note-UNWRAbomb-496]

#### Intimidation of journalists

Israeli officials said Hamas intimidates journalists in Gaza. A French reporter said that he was "detained and interrogated by members of Hamas's al-Qassam Brigade" in Gaza's Al-Shifa hospital, and forced to leave Gaza; he later asked the newspaper to remove his article from their site.[^cite_note-Timesintimidation-414][^cite_note-497][^cite_note-498] Some journalists reported threats on social media against those who tweet about rocket launch sites. John Reed of *[The Financial Times](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Financial_Times)* was threatened after he tweeted about rockets being fired from near Al-Shifa Hospital, and [RT](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/RT_\(TV_network\)) correspondent Harry Fear was told to leave Gaza after he tweeted that Hamas fired rockets from near his hotel.[^cite_note-Timesintimidation-414] [Isra al-Modallal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Isra_al-Modallal), head of foreign relations for the Hamas Information Ministry, said Hamas did deport foreign journalists who filmed Hamas rocket launches, stating that by filming the launch sites the journalists were collaborating with Israel.[^cite_note-499][^cite_note-500] The Foreign Press Association (FPA) in Israel and the Palestinian territories protested what it called "blatant, incessant, forceful and unorthodox methods employed by the Hamas authorities ... against visiting international journalists in Gaza", saying several had been harassed or questioned over information they reported. It also said that Hamas was trying to "put in place a 'vetting' procedure" that would allow the blacklisting of specific journalists.[^cite_note-501][^cite_note-502] *[The Jerusalem Post](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Jerusalem_Post)* said UNRWA workers were threatened by Hamas at gun-point during the war, but [Christopher Gunness](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christopher_Gunness), UNRWA spokesman, said "I have checked and double checked with sources in Gaza and there is no evidence of death threats made to UNRWA personnel."[^cite_note-503]

Some FPA members disputed the FPA's comments, including *[New York Times](30680.md)* Jerusalem bureau chief Jodi Rudoren, who wrote "every reporter I've met who was in Gaza during \[the] war says this Israeli/now FPA narrative of Hamas harassment is nonsense."[^cite_note-504] *[Haaretz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Haaretz)* interviewed many foreign journalists and found "all but a few of the journalists deny any such pressure". They said Hamas's intimidation was no worse than what they got from the IDF, and said no armed forces would permit reporters to broadcast militarily sensitive information; and that, furthermore, most reporters seldom saw Hamas fighters, because they fought from concealed locations and in places that were too dangerous to approach.[^cite_note-505]

### Alleged violations by Israel

Israel received some 500 complaints concerning 360 alleged violations. 80 were closed without criminal charges, 6 cases were opened on incidents allegedly involving criminal conduct, and in one case regarding 3 IDF soldiers in the aftermath of the [Battle of Shuja'iyya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Shuja'iyya_\(2014\)), a charge of looting was laid. Most cases were closed for what the military magistrates considered to be lack of evidence to sustain a charge of misconduct. No mention was made of incidents during the "Black Friday" events at Rafah.[^cite_note-506][^cite_note-507]

According to [Assaf Sharon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Assaf_Sharon?action=edit\&redlink=1) of Tel Aviv University, the IDF was pressured by politicians to unleash unnecessary violence whose basic purpose was 'to satisfy a need for vengeance,' which the politicians themselves tried to whip up in Israel's population.[^cite_note-Sharon-41] [Asa Kasher](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asa_Kasher) wrote that the IDF was pulled into fighting "that is both strategically and morally asymmetric" and that like any other army it made mistakes, but the charges it faces are "grossly unfair".[^cite_note-Kasher-425] The Israeli NGO [Breaking the Silence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Breaking_the_Silence_\(non-governmental_organization\)), reporting on its analysis of 111 testimonies concerning the war by some 70 IDF soldiers and officers,[^cite_note-GordonLRB-508][^cite_note-509] cited one veteran's remark that "Anyone found in an IDF area, which the IDF had occupied, was not a civilian," to argue that this was the basic rule of engagement. Soldiers were briefed to regard everything inside the Strip as a threat. The report cites several examples of civilians, including women, being shot dead and defined as "terrorists" in later reports.[^cite_note-510][^cite_note-511] Since leaflets were dropped telling civilians to leave areas to be bombed, soldiers could assume any movement in a bombed area entitled them to shoot.[^cite_note-GordonLRB-508] In one case that came under investigation, Lt Col Neria Yeshurun ordered a Palestinian medical centre to be shelled to avenge the killing of one of his officers by a sniper.[^cite_note-512]

#### Civilian deaths

Many of those killed were civilians, prompting concern from many humanitarian organisations. An investigation by [Human Rights Watch](46530.md) concluded that Israel had probably committed war crimes on three specific incidents involving strikes on UNWRA schools.[^cite_note-513] Amnesty International stated that: "Israeli forces have carried out attacks that have killed hundreds of civilians, including through the use of precision weaponry such as drone-fired missiles, and attacks using munitions such as artillery, which cannot be precisely targeted, on very densely populated residential areas, such as Shuja'iyya. They have also directly attacked civilian objects."[^cite_note-amnesty1-372] [B'tselem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/B'tselem) has compiled an infogram listing families killed at home in 72 incidents of bombing or shelling, comprising 547 people killed, of whom 125 were women under 60, 250 were minors, and 29 were over 60.[^cite_note-btselem-families-514] On 24 August, Palestinian health officials said that 89 families had been killed.[^cite_note-515]

Nine people were killed while watching the [World Cup](656933.md) in a cafe,[^cite_note-516] and 8 members of a family died that Israel has said were inadvertently killed.[^cite_note-517] A [Golani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Golani_Brigade) soldier interviewed about his operations inside Gaza said they often could not distinguish between civilians and Hamas fighters because some Hamas operatives dressed in plainclothes and the night vision goggles made everything look green. An IDF spokesperson said that Hamas "[deploys in residential areas, creating rocket launch sites, command and control centers, and other positions deep in the heart of urban areas](#Use_of_civilian_structures_for_military_purposes). By doing so, Hamas chooses the battleground where the IDF is forced to operate."[^cite_note-518] The highest-ranking U.S. military officer, Army General [Martin Dempsey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martin_Dempsey), the chairman of the Joint Chiefs of Staff, said that "Israel went to extraordinary lengths to limit collateral damage and civilian casualties". Later in his speech he said, "the Pentagon three months ago sent a 'lessons-learned team' of senior officers and non-commissioned officers to work with the IDF to see what could be learned from the Gaza operation, to include the measures they took to prevent civilian casualties and what they did with tunneling."[^cite_note-519] Col. [Richard Kemp](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Richard_Kemp) told *[The Observer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Observer.com)*, "IDF has taken greater steps than any other army in the history of warfare to minimise harm to civilians in a combat zone"[^cite_note-520]

##### Warnings prior to attacks

In many cases the IDF warned civilians prior to targeting militants in highly populated areas in order to comply with international law.[^cite_note-washingtonpost1-369][^cite_note-521][^cite_note-522][^cite_note-523] Human rights organizations including [Amnesty International](18947898.md),[^cite_note-amnestyQandA-524][^cite_note-independentRoofKnocking-525] confirmed that in many cases, Palestinians received warnings prior to evacuation, including flyers, phone calls and [roof knocking](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roof_knocking). A report by [Jaffa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jaffa) based NGO [Physicians for Human Rights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Physicians_for_Human_Rights%E2%80%93Israel), released in January 2015, said that Israel's alert system had failed, and that the roof-knock system was ineffective.[^cite_note-526] The IDF was criticized for not giving civilians enough time to evacuate.[^cite_note-527] In one case, the warning came less than one minute before the bombing.[^cite_note-528] Hamas has told civilians to return to their homes or stay put following Israeli warnings to leave.[^cite_note-529] In many cases, Palestinians evacuated; in others, they have stayed in their homes. Israel condemned Hamas's encouragement of Palestinians to remain in their homes despite warnings in advance of airstrikes.[^cite_note-CNN-crisis-247] Hamas stated that the warnings were a form of psychological warfare and that people would be equally or more unsafe in the rest of Gaza.[^cite_note-CNN-crisis-247][^cite_note-northern-530]

Amnesty International said that "although the Israeli authorities claim to be warning civilians in Gaza, a consistent pattern has emerged that their actions do not constitute an "effective warning" under international humanitarian law."[^cite_note-amnesty1-372] Human Rights Watch concurred.[^cite_note-hrwUnlawfulAistrikes-446] Many Gazans, when asked, told journalists that they remained in their houses simply because they had nowhere else to go.[^cite_note-northern-530] OCHA's spokesman has said "there is literally no safe place for civilians" in Gaza.[^cite_note-531] Roof knocking has been condemned as unlawful by Amnesty International[^cite_note-independentRoofKnocking-525] and Human Rights Watch[^cite_note-washingtonpost1-369] as well as the [United Nations Fact Finding Mission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Fact_Finding_Mission_on_the_Gaza_Conflict) in the [2008 war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaza_War_\(2008%E2%80%9309\)).

#### Destruction of homes



Ruins of a residential area in [Beit Hanoun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beit_Hanoun)

See also: [§ Use of civilian structures for military purposes](#Use_of_civilian_structures_for_military_purposes), and [House demolition in the Israeli-Palestinian conflict](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/House_demolition_in_the_Israeli-Palestinian_conflict)

Israel targeted many homes in this conflict. UNWRA official Robert Turner estimated that 7,000 homes were demolished and 89,000 were damaged, some 10,000 of them severely.[^cite_note-532] This has led to many members of the same family being killed. B'Tselem documented 59 incidents of bombing and shelling, in which 458 people were killed.[^cite_note-btselem-families-514] In some cases, Israel has stated that these homes were of suspected militants and were [used for military purposes](#Use_of_civilian_structures_for_military_purposes). *The New York Times* noted that the damage in this operation was higher than in the previous two wars and stated that 60,000 people had been left homeless as a result.[^cite_note-NYTGazaEconomy-324] The destruction of homes has been condemned by B'Tselem,[^cite_note-btselem1-367] [Human Rights Watch](46530.md)[^cite_note-indiscriminate-368][^cite_note-usatoday.com-447] and Amnesty International[^cite_note-amnestyQandA-524] as unlawful, amounting to collective punishment and [war crimes](48626.md).

Israel destroyed the homes of two suspects in the case of the abduction and killing of the three teenagers. The house demolition has been condemned by B'Tselem as unlawful.[^cite_note-533]

Palestinians returning to their homes during the ceasefire reported that IDF soldiers had trashed their homes, destroyed home electronics such as TV sets, spread feces in their homes, and carved slogans such as "Burn Gaza down" and "Good Arab = dead Arab" in walls and furniture. The IDF did not respond to a request by *[The Guardian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Guardian)* for comment.[^cite_note-534]

On 5 November 2014, Amnesty International published a report examining eight cases where the IDF targeted homes, resulting in the deaths of 111 people, of whom 104 were civilians. Barred from access to Gaza by Israel since 2012, it conducted its research remotely, supported by two contracted Gaza-based fieldworkers who conducted multiple visits of each site to interview survivors, and consulted with military experts to evaluate photographic and video material. It concludes, in every case, that "there was a failure to take necessary precautions to avoid excessive harm to civilians and civilian property, as required by international humanitarian law" and "no prior warning was given to the civilian residents to allow them to escape." As Israel did not disclose any information regarding the incidents, the report said it was not possible for Amnesty International to be certain of what Israel was targeting; it also said that if there were no valid military objectives, international humanitarian law may have been violated, as attacks directed at civilians and civilian objects, or attacks which are otherwise disproportionate relative to the anticipated military advantage of carrying them out, constitute war crimes.[^cite_note-535][^cite_note-536]

The report was dismissed by the Israeli Ministry of Foreign Affairs as "narrow", "decontextualized", and disattentive of alleged war crimes perpetrated by Hamas. Amnesty, it asserted, was serving as "a propaganda tool for Hamas and other terror groups."[^cite_note-537][^cite_note-538] Anne Herzberg, legal adviser for NGO Monitor, questioned the accuracy of the [UN](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UN) numbers used in the report, saying that they "essentially come from Hamas."[^cite_note-539]

#### Shelling of UNRWA schools

This section is an excerpt from [2014 Israeli shelling of UNRWA Gaza shelters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_Israeli_shelling_of_UNRWA_Gaza_shelters).\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=2014_Israeli_shelling_of_UNRWA_Gaza_shelters\&action=edit)]

There were seven [shellings](337004.md) at [UNRWA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Relief_and_Works_Agency_for_Palestine_Refugees_in_the_Near_East) facilities in the [Gaza Strip](12047.md) which took place between 21 July and 3 August 2014. The incidents were the result of [artillery](2508.md), [mortar](324499.md) or aerial missile fire which struck on or near the UNRWA facilities being used as shelters for [Palestinians](23267.md), and as a result at least 44 civilians, including 10 UN staff, died. During the 2014 Israel-Gaza conflict, many Palestinians fled their homes after warnings by Israel or due to air strikes or fighting in the area. An estimated 290,000 people (15% of Gaza's population) took shelter in UNRWA schools.&#x20;

On three occasions, on 16 July,[^cite_note-2014_Israeli_shelling_of_UNRWA_Gaza_shelters_UNRWA-540] 22 July[^cite_note-2014_Israeli_shelling_of_UNRWA_Gaza_shelters_unrwa.org-541] and on 29 July, UNRWA announced that rockets had been found in their schools.[^cite_note-2014_Israeli_shelling_of_UNRWA_Gaza_shelters_By_Nidal_al-Mughrabi_and_Maayan_Lubell-542] UNRWA denounced the groups responsible for "flagrant violations of the neutrality of its premises". All of these schools were vacant at the time when rockets were discovered; no rockets were found in any shelters which were shelled. The [Israel Defense Forces](39237.md) (IDF) stated that "[Hamas](13913.md) chooses where these battles are conducted and, despite Israel's best efforts to prevent civilian casualties, Hamas is ultimately responsible for the tragic loss of civilian life. There has been repeated abuse of UN facilities by Hamas, with at least three cases of munitions storage within such facilities."[^cite_note-2014_Israeli_shelling_of_UNRWA_Gaza_shelters_guardianUNRWAcloser-543]

The attacks were condemned by members of the [UN](31769.md) (UNRWA's parent organization) and other governments, such as the U.S., have expressed "extreme concern" over the safety of Palestinian civilians who "are not safe in UN-designated shelters."[^cite_note-2014_Israeli_shelling_of_UNRWA_Gaza_shelters_guardianUNRWAcloser-543] The [Rafah shelling](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_Israeli_shelling_of_UNRWA_Gaza_shelters#Rafah_Preparatory_A_Boys_School) in particular was widely criticized, with [Ban Ki-moon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ban_Ki-moon) calling it a "moral outrage and a criminal act" and US State Department calling it "appalling" and "disgraceful". UN High Commissioner for Human Rights Navi Pillay said that both Hamas militants and Israel might have committed war crimes. A Human Rights Watch investigation into three of the incidents concluded that Israel committed war crimes because two of the shellings "did not appear to target a military objective or were otherwise indiscriminate", while the third Rafah shelling was "unlawfully disproportionate".[^cite_note-2014_Israeli_shelling_of_UNRWA_Gaza_shelters_reutersHRW-544] On 27 April 2015, the United Nations released an inquiry which concluded that Israel was responsible for the deaths of at least 44 Palestinians who died in the shelling and 227 were injured.[^cite_note-545]

#### Infrastructure

On 23 July, twelve human rights organizations in Israel released a letter to the Israeli government warning that "Gaza Strip's civilian infrastructure is collapsing".[^cite_note-546][^cite_note-547] They wrote that "due to Israel's ongoing control over significant aspects of life in Gaza, Israel has a legal obligation to ensure that the humanitarian needs of the people of Gaza are met and that they have access to adequate supplies of water and electricity." They note that many water and electricity systems were damaged during the conflict, which has led to a "pending humanitarian and environmental catastrophe". The *[Sydney Morning Herald](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sydney_Morning_Herald)* reported that "almost every piece of critical infrastructure, from electricity to water to sewage, has been seriously compromised by either direct hits from Israeli air strikes and shelling or collateral damage."[^cite_note-548]



The only [power plant of Gaza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Power_plant_of_Gaza) after it was attacked by the Israeli army on July 14, 2014

Between five and eight of the 10 power lines that bring electricity from Israel were disabled, at least three by Hamas rocket fire.[^cite_note-549][^cite_note-550][^cite_note-551] On 29 July, Israel was reported to bomb Gaza's only power plant,[^cite_note-552] which was estimated to take a year to repair. Amnesty International said the crippling of the power station amounted to "collective punishment of Palestinians".[^cite_note-553][^cite_note-554] Human Rights Watch has stated that "\[d]amaging or destroying a power plant, even if it also served a military purpose, would be an unlawful disproportionate attack under the laws of war".[^cite_note-555] Israel immediately denied damaging the power plant, stating there was "no indication that \[IDF] were involved in the strike ... The area surrounding the plant was also not struck in recent days."[^cite_note-556] Contradicting initial reports that it would take a year to repair, the power plant resumed operation on 27 October.[^cite_note-557][^cite_note-558]

#### Attacks on journalists

17 journalists were killed in the conflict,[^cite_note-559][^cite_note-JPostFeb2015-560] of which five were off-duty and two (from [Associated Press](18935732.md)) were covering a bomb disposal team's efforts to defuse an unexploded Israeli artillery shell when it exploded.[^cite_note-561][^cite_note-alJazeeraJournalists-562] In several cases, the journalists were killed while having markings distinguishing them as press on their vehicles or clothing.[^cite_note-563][^cite_note-564] IDF stated that in one case it had precise information that a vehicle marked "TV" that was hit killing one alleged journalist was in military use.[^cite_note-IDFMAG-565][^cite_note-566] Several media outlets, including the offices of *Al-Jazeera*, were hit. The [International Federation of Journalists](1178863.md) has condemned the attacks as "appalling murders and attacks".[^cite_note-567] Journalists are considered civilians and should not be targeted under international humanitarian law.[^cite_note-alHaqJournalist-568] The Israeli army said it does not target journalists, and that it contacts news media "in order to advise them which areas to avoid during the conflict".[^cite_note-alJazeeraJournalists-562] Israel has made foreign journalists sign a waiver stating that it is not responsible for their safety in Gaza, which [Reporters Without Borders](526054.md) calls contrary to international law.[^cite_note-569][^cite_note-570][^cite_note-571] The Director-General of [UNESCO](21786641.md), [Irina Bokova](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Irina_Bokova), who in August 2014 condemned the killing of [Al Aqsa TV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al_Aqsa_TV) journalist Abdullah Murtaja, withdrew her comments after it was revealed that Murtaja was also a combatant in Hamas's Al Qassam Brigade, and said she "deplore\[d] attempts to instrumentalize the profession of journalists by combatants"[^cite_note-572][^cite_note-573]

[ITIC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Intelligence_and_Terrorism_Information_Center) published a report analyzing a list of 17 names published by Wafa News Agency based on information originating from Hamas-controlled Gaza office of the ministry of information that supposedly belong to journalists killed in the operation. The report says that 8 of the names belong to Hamas or Islamic Jihad operatives, or employees of the Hamas media.[^cite_note-JPostFeb2015-560][^cite_note-574]

Israel bombed Hamas's Al-Aqsa radio and TV stations because of their "propaganda dissemination capabilities used to broadcast the messages of (Hamas's) military wing."[^cite_note-575] [Reporters Without Borders](526054.md) and [Al-Haq](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Haq) condemned the attacks, saying "an expert committee formed by the International Criminal Court's prosecutor for the former Yugoslavia, to assess the NATO bombing campaign of 1999, specified that a journalist or media organization is not a legitimate target merely because it broadcasts or disseminates propaganda."[^cite_note-alHaqJournalist-568][^cite_note-576] The U.S. government classifies Al-Aqsa TV as being controlled by Hamas, a "Specially Designated Global Terrorist," and states that it "will not distinguish between a business financed and controlled by a terrorist group, such as Al-Aqsa Television, and the terrorist group itself."[^cite_note-577][^cite_note-578][^cite_note-579]

#### Human shields

The UN High Commissioner for Human Rights [Navi Pillay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Navi_Pillay) accused Israel of having "defied international law by attacking civilian areas of Gaza such as schools, hospitals, homes and U.N. facilities. "None of this appears to me to be accidental," Pillay said. "They appear to be defying – deliberate defiance of – obligations that international law imposes on Israel.""[^cite_note-AP-warcrimes-370] The [Jaffa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jaffa) based NGO [Physicians for Human Rights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Physicians_for_Human_Rights%E2%80%93Israel) stated in a report in January 2015 that the IDF had used human shields during the war. IDF criticized the report's conclusions and methodology which "cast a heavy shadow over its content and credibility".[^cite_note-580] [Defense for Children International](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Defense_for_Children_International)-Palestine reported that 17-year-old Ahmad Abu Raida was kidnapped by Israeli soldiers who, after beating him up, used him as a [human shield](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_shield) for five days, forcing him to walk in front of them with police dogs at gunpoint, search houses and dig in places soldiers suspected there might be tunnels.[^cite_note-581][^cite_note-582] Several of the key claims could not be verified because his Hamas-employed father said he forgot to take photographs of the alleged abuse marks and discarded all the clothing IDF soldiers supposedly provided Abu Raida when he was freed.[^cite_note-NYT_Raida-583]

The IDF confirmed that the troops suspected Ahmad of being a militant based on the affiliation of his father (a senior official in Gaza's Tourism Ministry) with Hamas and so detained him during the ground operation. The IDF and Israeli authorities challenged the credibility of DCI-P noting their "scant regard for truth".[^cite_note-NYT_Raida-583] The IDF Military Advocate General opened criminal investigation into the event.[^cite_note-IDFMAG-565]

## Military operations, weaponry and techniques

### Gaza



Range of rockets launched from Gaza Strip



Palestinian militants with rockets

#### Rockets

The Gazan militants used different kinds of rockets, including the Syrian-made (Chinese-designed) M-302[^cite_note-584] and the locally made M-75, which had the range to hit Tel-Aviv.[^cite_note-585][^cite_note-586][^cite_note-587] Other rockets include the Soviet [Katyushas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Katyusha_rocket_launcher) and [Qassams](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qassam_rocket).[^cite_note-588] The Israeli Defense Force reported that at the beginning of the 2014 conflict, Hamas had close to 6,000 rockets in its possession. This included 1,000 self-produced short range rockets (15–20 km range), 2,500 smuggled short range rockets, 200 self-made Grad rockets, and 200 smuggled Grad rockets. In addition, to these short range rockets, Hamas held an assortment of mid and long range rockets, both self-made and smuggled, that totaled over several thousand.[^cite_note-589]

According to the [Fars News Agency](5000099.md), [Fajr-5](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fajr-5) (long range Iranian) rockets had a warhead of 150–200 kg.[^cite_note-590] According to [Theodore Postol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Theodore_Postol), the vast majority of Gazan artillery rocket warheads contained 10- to 20-pound explosive loads. Postol stated that this fact made bomb shelters more effective for protection.[^cite_note-Postol-explanation-591] [Mark Perry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mark_Perry_\(author\)) stated that the "vast majority of the rockets are unsophisticated Qassams, with a 10–20 kg warhead and no guidance system" and that "Hamas' arsenal is considerably weaker today than it was in 2012". Regarding the Fajr-5, he stated that Iran had not transferred full-fledged rockets to Hamas, it has only transferred technology to manufacture them. He also stated that "its guidance system was crude, at best, and its warhead nearly non-existent."[^cite_note-592]

The UNHRC, quoting Amnesty International, stated that armed groups in Gaza have used BM-21 Grad rockets with ranges varying from 20 km to 48 km, in addition to locally produced rockets reaching as far as 80 km, such as the M-75 and J-80. The majority of the rockets had no guidance system. Mortars having a range of up to 8 km, have been actively used along the Green Line. Other weapons included rocket-propelled grenades, home-made drones, SA 7 Grail anti-aircraft missiles, Kornet 9M133 anti-tank guided missiles, and a wide array of small arms, rifles, machine guns and hand grenades.[^cite_note-593]

According to the IDF, of all the 4,564 projectiles fired at Israel, 224 hit built-up areas, 735 were intercepted by the [Iron Dome](18342427.md), 875 [fell inside Gaza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_Israel%E2%80%93Gaza_conflict#Hamas_rockets_failing)[^cite_note-heres-what-really-happened-301] and the rest fell in open territory or failed to launch.[^cite_note-OPE-Israeli-wounded-17]

According to OCHA, Palestinian militants fired 4,844 rockets and 1,734 mortar shells towards Israel.[^cite_note-OCHA30Sept-594] 25% of Gazan rockets had sufficient effectiveness to threaten to reach populated areas.[^cite_note-595]

#### Gazan tunnels

See also: [Gaza Strip smuggling tunnels](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaza_Strip_smuggling_tunnels) and [Tunnel warfare](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tunnel_warfare)

This section is an excerpt from [Tunnel warfare in the Gaza Strip](43413500.md).\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Tunnel_warfare_in_the_Gaza_Strip\&action=edit)]



An Israeli soldier overlooking an uncovered Palestinian tunnel in the [Gaza Strip](12047.md) during 2014 Gaza War

A vast network of underground tunnels used for [warfare](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tunnel_warfare) and to avoid Israeli airstrikes exists in the [Gaza Strip](12047.md). This infrastructure runs throughout the Gaza Strip and towards [Egypt](8087628.md) and [Israel](9282173.md), and has been developed by [Hamas](13913.md) and other [Palestinian military organizations](2646043.md) to facilitate the storing and shielding of weapons; the gathering and moving of fighters, including for training and communication purposes; the launching of offensive attacks against Israel; and the transportation of Israeli hostages. On several occasions, Palestinian militants have also used this tunnel network, which is colloquially referred to as the [Gaza metro](43413500.md),[^cite_note-596][^cite_note-597][^cite_note-Tunnel_warfare_in_the_Gaza_Strip_themetro-598]‌ to infiltrate Israel and Egypt while masking their presence and activities within the Gaza Strip itself. According to Iranian military officer [Hassan Hassanzadeh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hassan_Hassanzadeh), who commanded the [Islamic Revolutionary Guard Corps](1285472.md) from [Tehran](57654.md), the Gaza Strip's tunnels run for more than 500 kilometres (310 mi) throughout the territory.[^cite_note-599]

#### Other weaponry

Hamas has also used a "crude, tactical" drone, reported to be Iranian-made and named "Ababil-1".[^cite_note-600]

Palestinian militant groups have also used anti-tank rockets against armoured vehicles, as well as against groups of Israeli soldiers. Some armored personnel carriers were hit by missiles,[^cite_note-markPerryWeapons-601][^cite_note-602] and the Israeli [Trophy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trophy_\(countermeasure\)) system reportedly intercepted at least 15 anti-tank missiles shot at [Merkava IV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Merkava_Mk._IV) tanks.[^cite_note-603] Anti-tank mines had also been used against armored vehicles.

### Israel



IDF Artillery Corps fires a 155 mm [M109 howitzer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M109_howitzer), 24 July 2014.

Israel used air, land and naval weaponry. The artillery includes Soltam M71 guns and US-manufactured Paladin M109s (155-mm howitzers).[^cite_note-markPerryWeapons-601] The aerial weaponry includes drones and [F-16](11642.md) fighter jets. Drones are used to constantly monitor the Gaza strip.[^cite_note-604][^cite_note-605] The IDF fired 14,500 tank shells and 35,000 other artillery shells during the conflict.[^cite_note-B'tselemJan2015-373]

The IDF stated that it attacked 5,263 targets in Gaza, including:[^cite_note-ynetnumbers-56][^cite_note-ITIC_2_sep-434]

- 1,814 rocket and mortar launch or otherwise related sites

- 191 weapon factories and warehouses

- 1,914 command and control centers

- 237 government institutions supporting the militant activity

- hundreds of military outposts inside buildings

According to OCHA figures, Israel fired 5,830 missiles in 4,028 [IAF](177619.md) air raids, the [IDF](39237.md)'s ground forces shot off 16,507 artillery and tank projectiles, and the [Israeli navy](1370788.md)'s off-shore fleet fired 3,494 naval shells, into the Gaza Strip.[^cite_note-OCHA30Sept-594]

Overall, Israel fired 34,000 unguided shells into Gaza. Of these 19,000 were high-explosive artillery shells, marking a 533% rise in the launching of artillery ordnance compared to Operation Cast Lead. Shelling of civilian areas with [155 mm (6.1 in)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/155mm) shells using Doher howitzers, with a kill radius of 150 yards (140 m), also increased.[^cite_note-606][^cite_note-607]

According to Palestinian authorities, 8,000 bombs and 70,000 artillery shells, or 20,000 tons of explosives (the equivalent of two low-yield tactical nuclear weapons), had been dropped on Gaza.[^cite_note-608][^cite_note-609][^cite_note-610][^cite_note-611] The Sydney Morning Herald quoted an anonymous expert who estimated that 10,000 tonnes of explosives were dropped from the air alone, which does not include tank and artillery shells.[^cite_note-612]

Between 32 and 34[^cite_note-ynetnumbers-56] known tunnels were destroyed or neutralized, 13 of them destroyed completely.[^cite_note-ITIC_2_sep-434]

The performance of the Iron Dome defense system was considered effective, achieving an almost 90% success rate.[^cite_note-613] Israel's early warning sirens and extensive [shelters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Air-raid_shelter) have been an effective defense against Gazan rocketry.[^cite_note-Postol-explanation-591] They are less effective against short-range mortars because the residents have less time to react.[^cite_note-614]

## Media and diplomacy

### Media coverage

Main article: [Media coverage of the 2014 Israel–Gaza conflict](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Media_coverage_of_the_2014_Israel%E2%80%93Gaza_conflict)

Portrayals of the conflict in different media outlets varied. U.S. news sources were often more sympathetic to Israel while British news sources featured more criticism of Israel.[^cite_note-615] Commentators on both sides claimed that the media was biased either for or against Israel.[^cite_note-616] According to an article by Subrata Ghoshroy published in the [Bulletin of the Atomic Scientists](100091.md), most United States media focused on Hamas rockets, of which only 3% actually strike populated areas (causing little damage), with less attention paid to Palestinian casualties, or to why Gazans back Hamas's rocket campaign.[^cite_note-617] As the conflict progressed and Palestinian deaths increased, British media became somewhat more critical of Israel.[^cite_note-618] Within Israel, the newspaper *[Haaretz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Haaretz)* issued an editorial stating that the "soft Gaza sand... could turn into quicksand" for the Israeli military and also warned about the "wholesale killing" of Palestinian civilians; the article declared: "There can be no victory here".[^cite_note-619] The *[Sydney Morning Herald](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sydney_Morning_Herald)* apologised for running an allegedly antisemitic cartoon after Australian Attorney-General [George Brandis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_Brandis) denounced it as "deplorable." Israel was accused of waging a propaganda war,[^cite_note-620] and on both sides, sympathetic authors [released video games relating to the conflict](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_Israel%E2%80%93Gaza_conflict_in_video_games).[^cite_note-621] In Israel, according to [Naomi Chazan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Naomi_Chazan), the Gaza war sparked "an equally momentous conflagration at the heart of Israeli society": attempts to question government policy were met with severe verbal and physical harassment, incidents of Arab-bashing occurred daily, and 90% of internet posts on the war were found to be racist or to constitute incitement.[^cite_note-622]

### Diplomatic efforts

&#x20;A number of diplomatic efforts were made to resolve the conflict. These attempts included efforts by [United States Secretary of State](32293.md) [John Kerry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Kerry) to broker a ceasefire between Israel and Hamas, like the meeting in Paris with [European G4](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/G4_\(EU\)) foreign ministers and his counterparts of [Qatar](8238258.md) and [Turkey](11125639.md).[^cite_note-623][^cite_note-624] Egypt brokered a number of ceasefires between Hamas and Israel.[^cite_note-625][^cite_note-626][^cite_note-627][^cite_note-628]



&#x20;[U.S. Secretary of State](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._Secretary_of_State) [John Kerry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Kerry) and Benjamin Netanyahu, [Tel Aviv](31453.md), 23 July 2014.&#x20;

### Israeli public diplomacy

Further information: [Israeli public diplomacy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_public_diplomacy)

#### Hamas is ISIS

*"Hamas is ISIS"* was first asserted by [Benjamin Netanyahu](56472.md) near the end of the 2014 Gaza War.[^cite_note-972_is-629] The comparison was criticized and mocked by some Israeli journalists.[^cite_note-tweet_Foley-630][^cite_note-972_is-629] Netanyahu was particularly criticised for including real ISIS propaganda in his social media posts promoting the "Hamas is ISIS" analogy, including photos of [James Foley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Foley_\(journalist\)) from an ISIS [beheading video](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beheading_video).[^cite_note-tweet_Foley-630][^cite_note-631][^cite_note-632]

Neyanyahu followed this by saying, *"Hamas is ISIS and ISIS is Hamas"*, in a 2014 speech at the [United Nations](31769.md).[^cite_note-why_dangerous-633][^cite_note-belal-634] In reference to this, the head of the Department of Political Science at [Hebron University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hebron_University),[^cite_note-belal-634] said it was "dangerous" to conflate Hamas and ISIS.[^cite_note-why_dangerous-633] Israeli journalists pointed out that Hamas more closely resemble the [Irgun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Irgun) and [Lehi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lehi_\(militant_group\))[^cite_note-635][^cite_note-972_is-629] more closely than Hamas resemble ISIS.[^cite_note-972_is-629]

Netanyahu resurrected this slogan a decade later. In the first days of the [Israeli bombing of the Gaza Strip](76139149.md) in [October 2023](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_in_Palestine#October), [The Jerusalem Post](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Jerusalem_Post) quoted [Benjamin Netanyahu](56472.md) saying, "They are savages. Hamas is ISIS", the article then highlighted some alleged similarities in the groups' influences identified by Dr. Harel Chorev (from the *[Moshe Dayan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moshe_Dayan) [Center for Middle Eastern Studies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moshe_Dayan_Center_for_Middle_Eastern_and_African_Studies)* at [Tel Aviv University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tel_Aviv_University)).[^cite_note-636] Netanyahu included this assertion in a public addresses in the United States made alongside Secretary Antony J. Blinken, in the first week of the Israeli bombing of the Gaza Strip.[^cite_note-USDOS-637] Netanyahu said, "Hamas is ISIS, and just as ISIS was crushed, so too will Hamas be crushed".[^cite_note-USDOS-637] Then in 2024, Netanyahu was using the spurious analogy to suggest that a global coalition resembling the [War against the Islamic State](43517689.md).[^cite_note-638]

International military experts and mainstream international media pointed out major differences, particularly relating to [nationalism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islam_and_nationalism), [Shia Islam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Kerman_bombings), [Christianity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian_Christians), [democracy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islam_and_democracy), and [destruction of cultural heritage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Destruction_of_cultural_heritage_by_the_Islamic_State).[^cite_note-639][^cite_note-640][^cite_note-641][^cite_note-642][^cite_note-643] ISIS want a purely theocratic system of government without any element of democracy, and ISIS violently attack Christians, whereas Hamas participated in the [2006 Palestinian legislative election](38945340.md) and the Hamas-led [electoral list](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electoral_list) that won the election included a [Palestinian Christian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian_Christian) running for the Christian [reserved](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reserved_political_positions#Palestinian_Authority) seat in [Gaza City](55387.md).[^cite_note-644][^cite_note-645] [Talal Abu Zarifa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Talal_Abu_Zarifa), a leader from the [DFLP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/DFLP) (a secular faction [allied to Hamas](75192468.md)), said Israel was using the comparison to *"justify its annihilation of Palestinian people and bloodshed"*.[^cite_note-646] A few commentators pointed out some commonalities, such as that both are on the [list of designated terrorist groups](63578.md) in the [United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_designated_terrorist_organizations_in_the_United_States?action=edit\&redlink=1),[^cite_note-convo-647] and [United Kingdom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_designated_terrorist_groups_in_the_United_Kingdom?action=edit\&redlink=1),[^cite_note-648] but still stressed the groups' very different ideological goals.[^cite_note-convo-647] Only a few pro-government Israeli sources agreed that Hamas and ISIS are comparable.[^cite_note-649]

## Efforts to reconstruct Gaza

An international conference took place on 12 October 2014 in Cairo, where donors pledged US\$5.4 billion to the Palestinians with half of that sum being "dedicated" to the reconstruction of Gaza, which was more than the US\$4 billion Abbas first sought.[^cite_note-650] Japan pledged US\$100 million in January 2015.[^cite_note-651] The [EU](9317.md) pledged €450 million to rebuilding Gaza.[^cite_note-jpost-gericke-652]

As of 1 February 2015, only US\$125 million of the \$2.7 billion for reconstruction had been paid out, while tens of thousands of Gazans were still homeless. In February 2015, 30 international aid organizations including [UNRWA](32270.md), the [World Health Organization](33583.md) as well as NGOs such as Oxfam, ActionAid and Save the Children International released a statement saying that: "we are alarmed by the limited progress in rebuilding the lives of those affected and tackling the root causes of the conflict." They stated that "Israel, as the occupying power, is the main duty bearer and must comply with its obligations under international law. In particular, it must fully lift the [blockade](19224310.md) within the framework of [United Nations Security Council Resolution 1860](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Security_Council_Resolution_1860) (2009)".[^cite_note-653] Catherine Weibel, UNICEF's Communication Chief in Jerusalem said: "Four infants died from complications caused by the bitter cold in Gaza in January... All were from families whose houses were destroyed during the last conflict and were living in extremely dire conditions."[^cite_note-654]

Only one percent of the needed building material had been delivered. The mechanism agreed between Hamas and the Palestinian Authority, meant to allow delivery of such material, has not worked.[^cite_note-655]

Hamas spokesman blamed Israel for causing an electricity crisis. Israel provided 50,000 liters of fuel for generators running during blackouts and repaired three power lines damaged during storms within a week.[^cite_note-656]

On 15 September 2014, a Fatah spokesperson accused Hamas of misappropriating US\$700 million of funds intended to rebuild Gaza.[^cite_note-657][^cite_note-658] On 6 January Hamas spokesperson said that Palestinian national consensus government ministers admitted redirecting rebuilding funds to [PNA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian_National_Authority) budget.[^cite_note-659] Israel's military estimated that 20% of cement and steel allowed by Israel to be delivered to Gaza for the reconstruction efforts were taken by Hamas.[^cite_note-660] Arne Gericke, a member of the [European Parliament](9581.md) said "It would sicken most \[European] taxpayers to know that the EU itself could be directly contributing to the tragic cycle of violence".[^cite_note-jpost-gericke-652]

The dramatic spike in rocket attacks is likely to put significant pressure on Israeli Prime Minister Benjamin Netanyahu to heed calls for an all-out offensive against the Islamist movement, which Israel and the US consider a terrorist organization. While there has been intermittent rocket fire from Gaza since the cease-fire that ended the November 2012 Pillar of Defense conflict, Israel has credited Hamas with largely doing its best to keep the various militant factions in line. Today, however, Hamas took direct responsibility for the fire for the first time, sending a barrage of dozens of rockets into Israel in the worst day of such violence in two years."

[Archived](https://web.archive.org/web/20240602215109/https://content.time.com/time/world/article/0,8599,1154653,00.html) from the original on 2 June 2024. Retrieved 2 June 2024.

[^cite_note-79]: &#x20;Nathan Thrall,['Our Man in Palestine,'](http://www.nybooks.com/articles/archives/2010/oct/14/our-man-palestine/) [Archived](https://web.archive.org/web/20151016073306/http://www.nybooks.com/articles/archives/2010/oct/14/our-man-palestine/) 16 October 2015 at the [Wayback Machine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wayback_Machine) [The New York Review of Books](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_New_York_Review_of_Books), 14 October 2010: 'Dayton, meanwhile, was overseeing the recruitment, training, and equipping of Abbas's rapidly expanding security forces. Khaled Meshaal, chief of Hamas's politburo, delivered a fiery speech denouncing "the security coup" as a "conspiracy" supported by "the Zionists and the Americans"—charges Fatah denied. In February 2007, on the brink of civil war, Fatah and Hamas leaders traveled to Mecca, where they agreed to form a national unity government, a deal the US opposed because it preferred that Fatah continue to isolate Hamas. Fayyad became finance minister in the new government, despite, he says, American pressure not to join. The Peruvian diplomat Alvaro de Soto, former UN envoy to the Quartet, wrote in a confidential "End of Mission Report" that the violence between Hamas and Fatah could have been avoided had the US not strongly opposed Palestinian reconciliation. "The US", he wrote, "clearly pushed for a confrontation between Fateh and Hamas."'

After seven years of a bitter and at times lethalrivalry between the two main Palestinian factions, Fatah and Hamas, a historic Palestinian unity government has been sworn in, ending years of division.
